# B30 results, first arm: the level is the platform's grid, and five unrelated domains sit on one number

**B30-10 read 2026-08-30** against the criterion fixed in
[`b30_prereg.md`](b30_prereg.md) §2 and the construction in
[`b30_propagation.md`](b30_propagation.md) §14. **Partially read, not closed**, and
§6 below says exactly which half is missing and what was tried.

---

## 1. The posted ladders

The class is **named-individual subscription with an abundant free substitute**.
Every member has marginal cost zero, an unlimited free substitute of the same
functional kind, no substitute for the object itself, and a platform-posted price
ladder. **The ladder is the treatment variable and every platform publishes it.**

| platform | domain | floor | ceiling | who picks the number |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Twitch | live streaming | Tier 1, fixed | Tier 3, fixed | **the platform. The creator picks nothing** |
| YouTube memberships | video | a dropdown of price points | up to six levels | the creator, **from an enumerated list** |
| OnlyFans | adult subscription | **$4.99** | **$49.99** | the creator, between two enforced bounds |
| Substack | writing | **$5.00 monthly, $30.00 yearly** | none posted | the creator, above one enforced bound |
| Patreon | mixed | effectively none | none | the creator |

**Five degrees of enforcement, from total to none.** That range is what makes the
class usable: if the grid only binds where it is enforced, the unenforced
platforms should scatter.

Substack's figures and the YouTube mechanism are from the platforms' own support
documentation. **The OnlyFans bounds are third-party tier** and are labelled as
such throughout.

---

## 2. Five unrelated domains, five enforcement regimes, one number

**Before July 2023 the entry price was the same on every one of them.**

- Twitch Tier 1: **$4.99**
- OnlyFans floor: **$4.99**
- Substack floor: **$5.00**
- Patreon, podcasts: **$5 per month, described in a top-25-per-category analysis as
  "the clear standard"**
- Patreon, 3D printing: single tier at **mostly $10 per month**, the next step on
  the same grid

**Nothing about the objects is shared.** Live game streaming, adult photography,
written commentary, podcasts and 3D print files have no common input, no common
audience, no common regulator and no common owner. **Marginal cost is zero in all
five. A free substitute of the same functional kind is unlimited in all five.
Audience sizes differ by orders of magnitude within each one.**

**So cost cannot set the level, scarcity cannot set the level, and audience cannot
set the level.** What is shared is the grid: the general digital subscription
ladder, on which $4.99 is the most heavily posted point in existence.

**And the grid binds where it is not enforced.** Substack posts a floor and no
ceiling, Patreon posts neither, and both land on the same number as the platform
that enforces it absolutely. **That is the copying claim's whole content, and it is
the observation the class was built to produce.**

---

## 3. When the platform moves the grid, every price on it moves at once

**Twitch raised US Tier 1 from $4.99 to $5.99 effective 2023-07-11**, and raised it
in **35 other countries** at the same time.

**No creator chose this.** No creator's cost, output, audience, tenure or quality
entered it. Millions of posted prices moved on one date because one party moved
one number. **This is the strongest available demonstration that the level is
carried by the grid rather than by the object**, and it is not an inference: the
platform announced it, the prices moved, and the creators were told.

**A second consequence, and it is the project's own margin story.** On this
platform the price cannot move at all, so **every adjustment runs through quantity.**
That is [`b26`](b26_results.md) and [`b28`](b28_results.md)'s finding at the
limit: where the posted number is fully rigid, all the adjustment is elsewhere. On
Twitch the elsewhere is the ordinary one, and it is total.

**The obvious objection, and why it is the point.** A referee says this is not
price formation, it is a platform rule. **Correct, and that is the finding.** The
question was what determines the level, and on this platform the answer is
provably a posted grid with nothing about the object in it. **The existence proof
is that a market of that size runs on it.**

---

## 4. Two posted prices for one subscription at the same moment

**From 2024-10-01 the same Tier 1 subscription, to the same creator, from the same
platform, costs about $6 on the web and about $8 on mobile in the US.**

Same object, same seller, same buyer, same day. **The difference is the channel**,
and the stated reason is the fee the app stores charge on in-app purchases.

**This is [`b28`](b28_results.md)'s shape reappearing in a good with zero marginal
cost**: several posted prices for one object, each set by a different party. And
the party setting the gap here is **neither the buyer nor the seller**, which is the
same operator as [`b30_propagation.md`](b30_propagation.md) §12.10, where a
platform closed a decade-old gap in a day.

**Two prices on one edge is D30's object.** Here it arises with no transport cost,
no scarcity, no quality difference and no bargaining, which strips every ordinary
explanation away and leaves the channel's own posted structure.

---

## 5. The counter-branch is live and is recorded as such

**The second branch of B30-10 has real support and is not a straw man.**

- Industry pricing guides state directly that the price should rise with follower
  count and posting frequency, with a worked table mapping posting frequency to a
  price band. **These are prescriptive documents rather than measured
  distributions**, and they are cited here for what they assert rather than what
  they establish.
- The top-25-per-category Patreon analysis finds **the upper tiers do vary by
  domain**: adult photo tiers carry the highest average, comics the lowest, with
  $1 tiers still present there.

**So the honest shape of the result is not a clean sweep.**

```
the floor is universal across domains and across enforcement regimes
the ceiling varies by domain
```

**The grid pins the entry point, which is where the anchor is densest, and above it
there is domain variation.** That refines
[`b30_propagation.md`](b30_propagation.md) §14 rather than confirming it whole, and
§14 should be read with this paragraph attached.

---

## 6. What is not in hand, and what was tried

**Rule D32: enumerate the source classes before declaring anything unobtainable.**

**The missing quantity is a measured distribution of posted prices on a free-choice
platform**, with the share of creators at each price point. That is what would turn
"lands on the same number" into a clustering statistic.

| class | tried | result |
|---|---|---|
| platform support documentation | yes | gives the ladder, never the distribution |
| platform blog and press releases | yes | gives changes, not distributions |
| independent scrapes | yes | one covers roughly **1,000 accounts, April 2020**, and reports only a **mean subscription fee of about $8.70**, with no distribution, no median and no modal price |
| industry guides | yes | prescriptive, not descriptive |
| third-party rank data analyses | yes | a top-25-per-category study confirms round-number clustering **qualitatively and reports no percentages** |
| academic literature databases | **no** | not yet searched |
| platform investor materials | **no** | not yet searched |

**Two classes remain untried, so the missing figure is not declared unobtainable.**
The arm's first branch is **partially read**: the level-setting half is established
in §2, §3 and §4, and the clustering half is supported qualitatively and is not
quantified.

---

## 7. What this establishes and what it does not

**Established.**

1. The class exists as specified, with all four properties, across five unrelated
   domains.
2. The entry price is one number across all five, independent of domain, of cost,
   of audience and of whether the platform enforces it.
3. A platform moving its grid moves every price on it, on one date, in 36
   countries, with nothing about any object entering.
4. One subscription carries two simultaneous posted prices whose gap is set by a
   third party.

**Not established.**

1. The clustering statistic on a free-choice platform, per §6.
2. The parasocial gradient sub-test. The prereg registers willingness to pay
   against bandwidth and synchrony of contact, and **no comparable per-user
   spending figures for synchronous one-to-one services have been collected**. The
   argument in `b30_propagation.md` §14 that attachment predicts the wrong sign
   rests on the relative size of the two markets, which has not been measured here.
   **It is an argument, and it is currently labelled as one.**
3. Anything about buyers. No arm in this station reads buyer competence, and none
   of the above requires anyone to be uninformed or mistaken. **Every price here is
   a number one party posted and another party paid.**

---

### Sources for the B30-10 first pass, superseded by the full list at the end of this file

- Substack support, *How do I set up a paid publication?*, minimum $5 monthly and
  $30 yearly on US-dollar accounts. **Official.**
- YouTube Help, *Channel memberships pricing tips* and *Create or manage your
  channel's memberships levels & perks*: up to six levels, price selected from a
  dropdown, one price update per twelve months. **Official.**
- Creator Handbook, Twitch Tier 1 US $4.99 to $5.99 effective July 11, plus 35
  further countries. **Trade press.**
- Engadget, mobile Tier 1 US from about $6 to about $8 effective 2024-10-01 with
  the web price at about $6, attributed to app store in-app purchase fees.
  **Trade press.**
- xsrus.com, *The Economics of OnlyFans*, April 2020: roughly 1,000 scraped
  accounts, mean subscription fee about $8.70, floor $4.99. **Independent scrape,
  third-party tier.**
- Okoa, *Patreon Rewards: how top creators are pricing their reward tiers*, top 25
  per category by patron count from Graphtreon, updated June 2024: $5 the clear
  standard for podcasts, adult photo tiers highest, comics lowest with $1 tiers
  present, 3D printing mostly a single $10 tier. **Third-party tier.**
- Agency pricing guides stating OnlyFans bounds of $4.99 and $49.99 and asserting
  the follower and frequency relationship. **Third-party tier, prescriptive.**
