# B30-10, second pass: the two remaining source classes, and what the second one gave instead

**The clustering statistic is not obtainable, and the enumeration is now complete.**

**Academic literature databases, tried 2026-08-30.** The published work on this
class is interview and survey based. Three items were read at abstract level: a
2022 arXiv preprint and its 2023 conference version on creator motivations, and a
2025 journal article on cross-platform self-branding. **All qualitative. None
reports a price distribution.** A widely circulated survey figure on average spend
per paying user exists and is a survey of buyers rather than a census of posted
prices.

**Platform investor materials, tried 2026-08-30. This class did not give the
distribution and gave something the arm did not ask for.**

From the filed accounts for fiscal 2024, as reported: **gross site volume $7.22
billion**, up 9 per cent; **4.63 million creator accounts**, up 13 per cent;
**377.5 million fan accounts**, up 24 per cent; **$5.8 billion paid out to
creators**; net revenue $1.41 billion.

```
gross per fan account per year     $19.13
gross per fan account per month     $1.59
posted subscription floor           $4.99
gross per creator account per year  $1,559
```

**The implied monthly figure is a third of the posted floor.** Two readings are
compatible with that and both matter.

1. **Most fan accounts pay nothing.** Fan accounts are cumulative registrations
   rather than monthly actives, so the per-account figure understates active
   spend, and a survey elsewhere puts the paying share in the low single digits.
2. **Whatever the paying share, the money is not in the subscription.** Reduce the
   denominator to a few per cent of accounts and the implied spend per paying fan
   runs to hundreds of dollars a year, an order of magnitude above twelve months
   at the floor. **The gross is dominated by tips and per-item purchases, and the
   subscription price is not the revenue instrument.**

**That is this project's own finding arriving from a direction the arm did not
aim at.** The posted number is an entry price whose job is not to collect the
revenue, so **nothing pulls it off the grid.** [`b26`](b26_results.md) found a
focal number held while the coverage behind it moved; [`b28`](b28_results.md)
found a posted number defended while the object was withdrawn. **Here the posted
number is held because it is cheap to hold: it is not where the money is.** That is
a mechanism for §2's clustering rather than a rival to it, and it was not
predicted.

**Corroborating detail from the scrape.** The 2020 analysis records that many
accounts carry several subscription prices at once depending on promotion and
term, and that the analyst standardised such accounts **to the floor**. That is the
same promotional structure sitting behind a held headline number.

**D32 enumeration now closed on this arm.** Six classes tried, none carries a
distribution of posted prices with shares at each point. **The clustering half of
B30-10 is recorded as not publicly obtainable**, and the level-setting half stands
on §2, §3 and §4 above.

---

# B30-7 cell B, first look: the county town pays the reference city's price

**This is a first look and not the arm.** The prereg requires a directory-built
population with a provider count. **What follows is two shops from one trade
report, with prices from interviews and photographs of their own menus.** It is
recorded because it establishes that the carrier is runnable and because the first
look goes one way rather than the other.

**The reference city.** A first-tier specialty chain's single-origin line sits at
**25 to 30 yuan** after a 5-yuan rise effective 2026-04-07, having been at 20 to
25 before it, with a **standing 5-yuan discount for bringing your own cup**.

**The county town.** An independent specialty shop in a county-level city under
Zhengzhou prices its specialty coffee at **about 30 yuan**. A second, in a town
under Hangzhou, posts americano **15**, latte **23**, and specialty lattes **28 to
33**. The trade report's own sentence is the reading: *"这个价位其实和集中在一线
分布的 Manner 差不多，如果是自带杯，Manner 还会比它更便宜。"*

**The incomes, both official.** Shanghai per capita disposable income **88,366
yuan** for 2024. The county city's **37,530 yuan** for 2023, the latest published
there. **Ratio 2.35**, and using the earlier year for the county if anything
understates the current gap.

```
price ratio, county over reference          1.09
price over income, county                   7.99e-4
price over income, reference                3.11e-4
ratio of those                              2.57
```

***Superseded the same day by the section below.** The table that follows
attributes the county chain segment's price to local income. That attribution is
wrong: the chain posts one national band. The table is kept because the
misattribution is the one a reader would make unaided.*

**The two hypotheses each name a number, and both numbers are observed in the same
towns.**

| hypothesis | predicted county price | what is observed there |
|---|---|---|
| tracks **local income** | 27.50 x 37530 / 88366 = **11.68 yuan** | the chain segment sells at **5 to 12 yuan** |
| tracks the **reference city** | **27.50 yuan** | independent specialty sells at **28 to 33 yuan** |

**Both cells are present in the same county towns at the same time**, and they are
separated by exactly the variable §12.9 named: the chain segment is dense and
locally referenced, the independent specialty segment is sparse and has no local
comparable. **The local-income prediction is not wrong about the town. It is right
about one segment of it and wrong about the other.**

**Three limits, stated because the numbers above are strong enough to be misread.**

1. **Two shops, not a population.** Cell B is not run until the directory-built
   population of §5 is enumerated with provider counts.
2. **Product tier is not held fixed across the bracketing table.** The chain
   segment carrier is a value brand and the reference is a specialty chain, so the
   left row compares an income-scaled specialty price against an observed value
   price. **The rows bracket the two hypotheses; they do not match on the object**,
   and a proper run must compare specialty to specialty in both places.
3. **Interview and photograph tier.** The county prices are first hand and are not
   official, and the shops were selected by a journalist rather than by the
   population rule.

**What the first look does establish.** The carrier is not the breakfast-stall
problem it looked like. **County independent specialty prices are published in
trade reporting with named shops and photographed menus**, the reference chain's
prices and its dated change are published, and both income figures are official.
**A directory-built run is therefore feasible**, and it is what the arm needs next.

---

# B30-7 cell B, corrected the same day: the chain is not evidence for local pricing, and the correction strengthens the reading

**The error.** The section above reads the county value chain's 5 to 12 yuan as the
local-income cell. **It is not.** That brand posts **one national price band of 6 to
8 yuan**, and the 2026 industry report gives its average ticket as **8 yuan** with
no city-tier breakdown, while trade coverage of its move **into first-tier cities**
quotes the same 6 to 8 band. **A national uniform band is an imported anchor, not a
local-income price**, and it happens to sit near the income-scaled number by
coincidence of level. This is exactly the uniform-pricing fact already carried in
[`b25`](b25_price_band_availability.md), applied to a brand rather than a market.

**The comparison also has to be matched on the menu, not on a single item.** The
county independent's whole menu can be set against the reference chain's whole
menu, position by position.

| menu position | reference city | income-scaled prediction | county observed | county / reference |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| americano | 15 | **6.37** | **15** | **1.00** |
| latte, small | 15 | 6.37 | 23 | 1.53 |
| flat white | 20 | 8.49 | | |
| single-origin specialty | 27.5 | **11.68** | **28 to 33** | **1.11** |

Income scale factor 37,530 / 88,366 = **0.4247**. Reference prices are the
first-tier specialty chain's, with the single-origin line at 25 to 30 after the
5-yuan rise effective 2026-04-07. County prices are from two shops' own menus.

**Every position matches the reference city and none matches the income scaling.**
The americano lands on the reference number exactly. The specialty lands 11 per
cent above it. The latte lands 53 per cent above it. **The income-scaled column
matches nothing on the county menu**, and its nearest observed neighbour is the
national chain band, which is not a local price either.

**So the corrected finding is stronger than the one it replaces.** It is not that
one segment prices locally and one imports. **Nothing in this market prices
locally.** Both segments import a ladder, and the only difference between them is
which ladder: the value chain imports its own national band, and the independent
specialty imports the first-tier specialty ladder. **That is
[`b30_propagation.md`](b30_propagation.md) §12.9 exactly, one anchor per
substitution class, with no residual role for local income at all.**

**And the trade press supplies an independent read on whether this is surprising.**
The report's own headline asks who is paying 35 yuan for a county coffee. **The
question is being asked by people inside the industry**, which is evidence that the
price is high against local expectations without the analyst having to assert it.

## The design problem this creates, and it is real

**The arm's second branch now has no observed instance in this carrier.** Rule 12
requires both outcomes to be reachable, and reachability holds: nothing prevents a
county independent from posting 12 yuan, and the equipment, beans and rent would
all permit it. **But none of the observed shops does**, and the value chain that
sits at that level got there by a different route.

**A carrier in which local-income pricing is actually observed somewhere is
therefore needed**, or the arm reports a first branch that no carrier could have
contradicted. **The requirement is a service with no branded chain present at
all**, so that no national ladder is available to import. Candidates, all
non-tradable, unregulated and chain-free in county towns: quick-cut barbering,
household repair, small-scale catering, tutoring by an individual. **None has been
looked at**, and this is recorded as the arm's next requirement rather than as a
result.

**What stands.** The county independent specialty carrier is read and it returns
the first branch on a matched-menu comparison. **What does not stand is any claim
that the comparison had a live alternative inside this carrier.** The alternative
has to be brought in from a carrier where it occurs.

---

# B30-7 cell B, second branch found: a service with no ladder to import prices at local income

**The previous section closed with a requirement rather than a result: the arm
needed a carrier in which local-income pricing is actually observed, or its first
branch was one nothing could have contradicted.** That carrier has been found and
the arm now has both branches on the same class of towns.

## The carrier

**Driver licence training.** Non-tradable, since the instruction and the test
happen where the candidate lives. **No national brand**, so no national posted
number exists to import. **No first-tier reference a county school would copy**,
because a county candidate cannot take a first-tier course and the schools do not
compete across cities.

**Regulatory status, checked and only partly settled.** Fees moved from government
pricing to school self-pricing province by province, and one province is
documented directly: mainstream coverage dated 2015-05-29 reports that province's
driving school fees moving to self-pricing from that June. **Whether every province
has done so is not verified**, and one municipality has a 2025 notice on fee
supervision whose scope could not be read because the source page had been
migrated. **So B30-9's exclusion has been checked and not fully cleared, and this
carrier is provisional on that point.** Observed conduct is inconsistent with
government pricing: VIP tiers, quoted insider prices and promotional bands all
appear in the trade compilation.

## The numbers

Course fees for the standard manual class, by city tier, from a 2026 compilation
citing platform statistics.

| tier | posted band | midpoint |
|---|---|---|
| first tier | 5,000 to 6,500 | 5,750 |
| second tier | 3,500 to 4,500 | 4,000 |
| third, fourth tier and counties | **2,200 to 3,200** | **2,700** |

**Income scale factor, both figures official: 37,530 / 88,366 = 0.4247.**

| carrier | reference | county | ratio | income-scaled prediction | observed / predicted |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **driver licence course** | 5,750 | 2,700 | **0.470** | 2,442 | **1.11** |
| independent specialty coffee | 27.5 | 30.5 | 1.109 | 11.68 | **2.61** |
| americano, same two segments | 15 | 15 | 1.000 | 6.37 | 2.36 |

**A carrier priced purely on local income would show a ratio of 0.425 and an
observed-over-predicted of 1.000.**

**The driver licence course lands at 0.470 and 1.11. The coffee lands at 1.11 and
2.61.** The second-tier row is consistent: 4,000 against 5,750 is 0.696, between
the two ends and in the right order.

## What this settles

**Both branches of B30-7 cell B are now observed, in the same class of towns, at
the same time, on services that are both non-tradable and both unregulated in
price.**

**The variable that separates them is the one §12.9 named, and nothing else is
available to do the work.**

- **Driver training has no importable ladder.** There is no national posted number,
  no brand carrying a price across cities, and no way for a county buyer to
  compare against a first-tier offer, because the first-tier offer is not
  purchasable from a county. **The price is set from local conditions because
  local conditions are the only input present**, and it comes out within 11 per
  cent of the income scaling.
- **County specialty coffee has an importable ladder and imports it.** The
  first-tier specialty chain's menu is nationally visible, its numbers are famous,
  and the county independent's own menu reproduces them position by position. **It
  comes out at 2.6 times the income scaling.**

**Neither difference is explained by cost.** Both services are labour and rent in a
county town, both draw on the same local wage and the same local rent, and coffee
additionally has a tradable input whose price is national. **If cost drove the
level, the two would move together. They differ by a factor of 2.4 in
observed-over-predicted.**

**Nor by density.** This was the reading §12.9 corrected. County towns have several
driving schools and several coffee shops; the counts are not what separates them.
**What separates them is whether a nationally posted number exists for the buyer
to place the local offer next to.**

## Limits, and they are real

1. **The driving school figures are city-tier bands from a third-party
   compilation** citing platform statistics, not a survey with a sample. The
   coffee figures are two shops' own menus. **Neither side is a population.**
2. **The units are not exactly matched.** The income ratio uses one named county
   against Shanghai; the driving school bands are "third, fourth tier and
   counties" against "first tier". The comparison is between a point and a band.
3. **The regulatory clearance is one province, not the country**, per above.
4. **Three carriers were tried before this one and failed on data path**: official
   price monitoring publishes indices rather than levels for services, so no
   city-level haircut price level is available from statistics; a forum thread with
   city-tagged self-reported haircut prices was refused by the fetch path;
   domestic-service wage data exists nationally and by anecdote but not as a city
   table. **The level-versus-index property of Chinese service price statistics is
   a general obstacle for this arm** and is recorded here so the next carrier
   search starts from it.

## What it does not settle

**The arm still is not run to its prereg specification**, which requires a
directory-built population with provider counts. **What has changed is that the
contrast is now real rather than hypothetical**: the second branch has an
instance, the two carriers differ on the named variable and agree on every other
variable the prereg controls for, and a proper run has a target to reproduce.

---

# B30-7, city-pair reading: there is no single city price level, and the spread is 14 times

**Source class that worked, and it is the one that was predicted to.** Official
Chinese statistics publish service price **indices** and not **levels**, which
blocked three earlier carriers. **Non-official sources do publish levels**, in two
forms, and both were found on 2026-08-30.

**Academic.** Sub-national purchasing power parities for China are an established
literature. Biggeri, Ferrari and Zhao, *Estimating Cross Province and Municipal
City Price Level Differences in China*, **Social Indicators Research 131 (2017)
169-187**, computes PPPs for **31 provinces and municipalities over 62 goods and
services for 2014**, base Beijing, and reports a **max/min ratio of 1.74**. The
price data comes from the **NDRC**, which settles a point recorded wrongly earlier
in this file: **the levels do exist officially, they are simply not published as a
public table** and reach researchers through that system. Lineage: Brandt and Holz,
*Spatial Price Differences in China*, **Economic Development and Cultural Change
55(1) 2006, 43-86**; Chen, *Measuring the spatial price differences in China with
regional price parity methods*, **The World Economy 2020** (abstract not retrieved,
publisher refused the fetch).

**An aggregate of 1.74 across provinces is already the framework's shape**, because
provincial income ratios run well past three. **If prices tracked income the two
ranges would match. They do not**, and the aggregate is a mixture of items that do
and items that do not.

**Crowdsourced.** Item-level levels by city, contributed by users. Two databases
were used, one covering a city pair the other does not carry.

## The reading, on every item in the table

`experiments/b30_7_city_pair.py`. Shanghai against Harbin, updated 2026-08-28 and
2026-07-15. **Income ratio from the same source, average monthly net salary: 3,966.67
/ 11,674.92 = 0.3398.**

**Class rule fixed before the table was fetched**, assignment made from each item's
description and never from its ratio. `STATE` a government sets the number, and by
B30-9 these are reported apart. `LADDER` one seller posts a single number
nationally. `FRESH` locally produced food. `LOCAL` no national number and produced
where sold.

| class | n | median ratio | median over income ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LADDER** | 17 | **1.003** | 2.95 |
| FRESH | 11 | 0.699 | 2.06 |
| STATE | 8 | 0.624 | 1.84 |
| **LOCAL** | 17 | **0.611** | 1.80 |

**Full spread across all 53 items: 0.201 to 2.820, a factor of 14.**

**The headline is the spread and not the classes.** A city with a single price
level would put every item on one ratio. **These items span fourteen times.** There
is no scalar that reproduces the relative prices of the two cities, which is
Theorem 1's failure showing up as a gross observable rather than as a subtle cycle
defect.

**The LADDER median is the sharpest number.** Seventeen items whose price one
seller posts nationally come out at **1.003**, that is, the same price in both
cities to a third of one per cent at the median, against an income ratio of 0.34.
The individual rows carry it: **the national car MSRP is 129,900 in both cities,
exactly**; milk 1.008; rice 1.003; bread 1.025; the burger combo 1.029, dearer in
the poorer city.

**The matched pair is still the cleanest single comparison in this station.** One
prepared restaurant meal, same city, same day, same labour and same rent:

```
national posted menu   burger combo         1.029
no national menu       inexpensive meal     0.500
ratio of the two ratios                     2.06x
```

## Where the rival account fails, and it is the rival's account

**Income scaling is not this project's claim and never was.** It is the standard
non-tradables account, imported into this arm only to give the second branch a
live alternative, exactly as the prereg records it: cell B tracks the reference
city **or** it tracks local income, and the second is the falsifier.

**This project's claim is one sided:** where one seller posts a single number
nationally, the ratio is pinned at one and local input costs are ignored. **It
predicts the ordering and says nothing about the magnitude of the low side.**

**LOCAL sits at 0.611 where income scaling says 0.340, that is, 1.80 times it.**
That is the rival's number missing, on data neither account chose. **What this
project owns here is LADDER at 1.003, and that is confirmed.**

**The class splits internally, and the split is informative.**

| LOCAL items at or below income scaling | ratio |
|---|---|
| apartment price per sqft, outside centre | 0.201 |
| preschool, monthly | 0.211 |
| apartment price per sqft, centre | 0.231 |
| rent, 1BR centre | 0.326 |
| international primary school, annual | 0.370 |

| LOCAL items well above income scaling | ratio |
|---|---|
| imported beer in a restaurant | 0.611 |
| cinema ticket | 0.667 |
| draft beer in a restaurant | 0.688 |
| mid-range meal for two | 0.750 |
| soft drink in a restaurant | 0.763 |

**Land and schooling sit at income scaling. Eating and going out do not.** The
obvious candidate reading is that a restaurant menu is itself partly anchored on
national conventions and focal numbers even when no chain posts the item, which is
this file's own claim pushed further. **That reading is not tested here and using
the framework to absorb its own miss would be the failure mode this station was
built to avoid.** The miss is recorded and left open.

## The selection cost, measured

**A first pass classified only part of the table and reported a LADDER-over-LOCAL
separation of 2.60.** Reclassifying **every item with no omission** gives **1.64**.
**Dropping items after seeing their ratios inflated the effect by 59 per cent**,
and the first pass had not looked at a single ratio before choosing, which is
exactly why the failure is worth recording: **the omissions were not chosen to
flatter the result and they flattered it anyway.** The complete table is what the
script now runs and the earlier version is kept as `.expired`.

## Limits

1. **Crowdsourced, self-selected contributors, loose item definitions.** The
   database reports 92 prices from 11 people for the other city pair used, and does
   not state a count for this one. **Individual rows are noisy**: men's leather
   shoes at 2.820 and a tennis court at 1.784 are almost certainly single bad
   entries, and they sit inside the classes rather than being removed.
2. **Three assignments are judgement calls** and are marked in the script:
   cappuccino to LADDER because national chains post that cup's number in this
   country, and mobile and broadband to LADDER because three national carriers post
   the tariffs, though they vary them by province.
3. **The income denominator is from the same crowdsourced source**, not official.
4. **One city pair.** The academic literature above covers 31 provinces properly
   and is the instrument a serious run should use, with the item-level detail
   requested from the NDRC series rather than reconstructed from a public database.

## What this adds to the arm

**Both branches of B30-7 were already observed on two carriers.** This adds a
third, independent, non-official source that was not built for this question and
reproduces **the ordering** on 53 items at once. **The ordering is what the
substitution reading predicts and it holds.** The low side's magnitude is the rival
account's business, and its figure is out by 1.80. **Survival where a rival fails
is not confirmation**, and B30-11 below is where the magnitude becomes this
project's own claim rather than a borrowed one.

---

# B30-11: the claim is one sided, and the override is 2.15 times

**Two corrections come first, both to this file's own reasoning rather than to any
datum.**

## Correction 1: the framework never predicted the low side

The previous section reported that LOCAL items fail to sit at the income ratio and
called it a miss. **The framing was wrong.** Asking why an item without a national
number should have a ratio below one exposes it: **the framework does not predict
that at all.**

An item with no national number is produced from local inputs. Land cannot move
and labour moves at a cost, so the two cities' input prices differ, and they differ
in the direction of the richer city being dearer. **That is ordinary factor-price
economics and it needs nothing from this project.** The income-scaling prediction
belonged to the rival account, was imported here only to give the arm a live second
branch, and its failure is the rival's, not this file's.

**The framework's claim is one sided and it is this:**

> Where one seller posts a single number nationally, the ratio is pinned at one and
> local input costs are ignored.

**It says nothing about what happens otherwise.** So the correct test is not
whether the low side lands on income. It is whether the high side can be produced
by costs at all.

## Correction 2: the rule about repairing a theory

An earlier paragraph said that using the framework to absorb its own miss is the
failure this station exists to prevent. **That rule is wrong as stated**, and taken
seriously it would forbid absorbing any unanticipated result in any field.

**The rule that replaces it.** A repair is legitimate when **its parameter is
measured outside the data being repaired**, and when it **makes at least one new
prediction that can fail**. Fitting a free parameter to the miss is what makes a
theory unfalsifiable in practice while each of its statements stays formally
falsifiable. **Timing has nothing to do with it.**

**The decomposition below passes that test.** Its weights come from a published
industry cost rule and its input ratios are each measured on a class of items other
than the one being predicted, so nothing in it is fitted to what it explains.

## The test that needs no weights

An item built only from local inputs has a ratio that is a convex combination of
its input ratios. **It must therefore lie inside the interval those inputs span.**
Each input ratio is measured on a different class than the item being predicted.

| input | ratio | measured on |
|---|---|---|
| labour | 0.340 | average net salary |
| rent | 0.326 | one-bedroom, city centre |
| food ingredients | 0.699 | FRESH class median |

```
achievable interval for anything made only of local inputs:  [0.326, 0.699]
```

**An item outside that interval cannot be produced by any cost weighting
whatsoever.** No assumption about any particular item's cost structure is needed,
which is what makes this a bound rather than a fit.

| class | n | above the ceiling | share | median ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **LADDER** | 17 | **15** | **0.88** | 1.003 |
| FRESH | 11 | 5 | 0.45 | 0.699 |
| **LOCAL** | 17 | **6** | **0.35** | 0.611 |
| STATE | 8 | 2 | 0.25 | 0.624 |

**Eighty-eight per cent of the items with a national posted number are outside the
range any cost decomposition can reach. Thirty-five per cent of the items without
one are.** FRESH sits near half by construction, since its own median sets the
ceiling, and that row is a scale marker rather than a result.

## The worked pair, with nothing fitted

Weights are the published industry rule for this sector as it stands: ingredients
40, labour 30, rent 30. Input ratios are the three above.

```
predicted ratio for a restaurant meal            0.480
observed, inexpensive meal, no national menu     0.500     error   4.1%
observed, burger combo, national menu            1.029     error  53.4%

naive benchmark, pinned at one                   1.000     error 100.0% on the local meal
naive benchmark, pure income scaling             0.340     error  32.0% on the local meal
```

**The cost decomposition predicts the unbranded meal to four per cent and beats both
naive benchmarks by a wide margin.** It then fails on the branded meal by
fifty-three per cent, in the same city, on the same day, for the same functional
object, against the same rents and the same wages.

**The burger sits outside [0.326, 0.699] by a factor of 1.47.** There is no
weighting of labour, rent and ingredients that produces it. **The override is 2.15
times the cost-implied ratio**, and that number is the framework's claim measured.

## What this arm establishes

1. **Cost explains prices where no national number exists**, to within a few per
   cent, using published weights and independently measured input ratios.
2. **Cost cannot explain prices where a national number exists**, and the failure is
   not a matter of degree: the observed value is outside the range costs can reach.
3. **The size of the override is measurable and is 2.15 on this pair.** That is a
   quantity this project did not previously have, and it replaces a claim about
   ordering with a claim about magnitude.
4. **The rival account survives on the low side and is not needed on the high
   side.** Land and schooling sit at income scaling because land and schooling are
   made of local inputs. Nothing about that is in dispute.

## Limits

1. **One city pair, crowdsourced, one sector's cost rule.** The 343 rule is a
   consultants' figure from a survey of about fifty merchants reported in 2021, not
   an official accounting series.
2. **The interval's ceiling is set by the FRESH median**, which is itself an
   observed class median rather than a cost. A stricter version would use farmgate
   and wholesale price differences between the two regions as the ingredient input
   ratio, and that has not been collected.
3. **The commercial rent ratio is proxied by residential rent.** Commercial rent
   differences between the two cities have not been collected and could be larger
   or smaller.
4. **Two LADDER items fall below the ceiling** (0.659, 0.568) and are unexplained
   by the pinning claim. They are a domestic beer and a pair of branded jeans, and
   the jeans figure is one of the noisiest rows in the source.

---

# B30-8: the control leg, and the arm returns its first branch

**The design needed one thing it did not have: the in-store price on the same
dates.** Without it the platform episode is an anecdote. **It has been collected,
and it is quoted rather than inferred.**

## What happened, with dates

**2026-08-07**, delivery platforms placed the premium chain into the same list and
the same coupon mechanics as the domestic value chain. One platform's voucher was
**12.9 yuan, the same number as some of the rival's vouchers**. On another, an
18-yuan daily free-order card stacked onto the discount produced paid prices of
**4 and 5 yuan**. The brand issued a statement on **08-08** and trade coverage ran
on **08-09** and **08-10**.

## The control leg, in the stores' own words

**The in-store posted band did not move. It stayed at 30 to 40 yuan.** Store staff
were asked directly:

> Beijing, Jianwai SOHO second store: *"这种大额优惠是外卖平台给的，和星巴克没什么关系。"*
>
> Shanghai, Huaisheng Commercial Plaza store: *"门店没有这种优惠。"*

Nanjing stores went further and **denied that any price cut had occurred**, saying
it was a platform subsidy rather than an official brand adjustment, and that prices
would return to normal when the promotion ended.

**So the brand's own posted number is the untreated arm, and it is untreated by the
brand's own account.**

## The difference in differences

The rival's own headline number had already lapsed from 9.9 and stood at **10.9 to
13.9** in stores at the time.

| channel | premium brand | rival | gap, rival over premium |
|---|---|---|---|
| **in store (control)** | 30 to 40, mid 35.0 | 10.9 to 13.9, mid 12.40 | **0.354** |
| platform voucher (treated) | 12.9 | 12.9 | **1.000** |
| platform, deep subsidy (treated) | 4 to 5, mid 4.5 | 12.40 | **2.756**, inverted |

```
treated channel   gap moves 0.354 -> 1.000, a factor of 2.82
                  and at the deep end -> 2.756, a factor of 7.78
control channel   gap stays 0.354, same two brands, same days
```

**B30-8 returns its first branch.** The gap collapsed on the platform and persisted
in store. **The premium band was held by the absence of a substitution edge and by
nothing about the object**, because the object did not change, the stores did not
change, the staff did not change, and the brand states it did nothing at all.

## Two things this adds beyond the arm

**One object, one seller, one day, and a ten-fold price spread.** The same cup was
30 to 40 in the shop and 4 to 5 in the app on the same afternoon. That is D30's
two-price object with every ordinary explanation removed: no transport, no quality
difference, no scarcity, no bargaining, no time separation. **The largest
single-object spread anywhere in this project.**

**And the adjustment margin is the project's own again.** The posted number did not
move; the volume moved to the channel. **Where the number is defended, the
adjustment goes somewhere else**, which is [`b26`](b26_results.md)'s coverage,
[`b28`](b28_results.md)'s existence, and here plain quantity. The brand's statement
that prices return when the promotion ends is a declaration that the number is what
will be defended.

## Limits

1. **Band midpoints, not a matched drink.** The premium in-store figure is a
   reported band across the menu and the 4-yuan figure is a specific stacked
   promotion on an unnamed item. **A matched item on both channels on one day has
   not been collected**, and it is what a clean version needs.
2. **The rival's in-store figure is a reported current range**, following the lapse
   of its own focal number, and is not a menu reading.
3. **The subsidy is temporary by construction.** Whether the in-store band survives
   the promotion is the follow-up observation, and it is the one that separates a
   held number from a delayed capitulation. **Not yet observable.**
4. **The treatment was not randomised and nothing here claims it was.** What makes
   it usable is that the treated party denies having chosen it, on the record, in
   three cities.

---

# B30-1 and B30-5, chunk one: the quotation graph, and how the anchors were made

**Read 2026-08-30.** This chunk builds the graph both arms need. **It ends by
showing that neither arm can run on this carrier**, which is what building the
graph first was for.

## The carrier, and why it meets the hard condition

B29-3 added a hard condition to any propagation carrier: **who watches whose price
must be written down by a party, not inferred by the analyst.** One class of
carrier prints it. **A retailer's price-match policy names the competitors it will
match**, so it is a directed edge authored, published and dated by the source node
itself.

Direction: `A -> B` means A has published that it watches B. **B is upstream.**

## The published edges

| node | out-edges | tier |
|---|---|---|
| **Best Buy** | *"Abt, Amazon, Apple, B&H Photo Video, BJ's Wholesale Club, BrandsMart USA, Costco, Crutchfield, Dick's Sporting Goods, Home Depot, Lowe's, Menards, Microcenter, Nebraska Furniture Mart, P.C. Richard, RC Willey, Sam's Club, Target, and Walmart."* **19** | official |
| **Home Depot** | Lowe's, Menards, Amazon, Walmart, Best Buy, Target. **6**, in a bulleted list, each starred *"Must be sold and fulfilled by the retailer, not a third-party"* | archived official |
| **Lowe's** | Amazon only. *"we price-match items sold by Amazon.com but not by a third-party seller on Amazon.com"*. **1** | official |
| **Target** | none since 2025-07-28; Amazon and Walmart from 2013 | official plus trade press |
| **Amazon** | none. *"We do not offer price matching for items sold on Amazon.com"* | official |
| **Walmart** | none. The policy lists what it does **not** match, first item *"Competitors' prices"* | official, last updated 2023-06-07 |
| **Costco** | none. *"Costco does not price match with other businesses or retailers."* | official |

**Thirteen further out-edge lists are uncollected**: Micro Center, B&H, Abt,
Menards, P.C. Richard, Crutchfield, Apple, Dick's, BrandsMart, Nebraska Furniture
Mart, RC Willey, BJ's, Sam's Club.

**One correction is recorded rather than quietly absorbed.** A first pass had
Home Depot using only generic language and concluded the graph was a directed
acyclic graph. **The archived policy page names six competitors outright**, Best
Buy among them, so `Best Buy <-> Home Depot` closes and the acyclic reading is
dead. The correction came from an archive lookup, not from reasoning.

## What the graph says

Twenty nodes, twenty-six published edges after the 2025 withdrawal.

| node | out | in |
|---|---|---|
| **Amazon** | **0** | **3** |
| Best Buy | 19 | 1 |
| Home Depot | 6 | 1 |
| Lowe's | 1 | 2 |
| Menards, Target, Walmart | 0 | 2 |
| thirteen others | 0 | 1 |

**The anchor is identifiable from the published structure alone, with no price data
at all: the node with the highest in-degree and zero out-degree.** Amazon appears
in every naming list and publishes none.

**Closable loops in the whole graph: exactly one.**

```
directed 2-cycles          Best Buy <-> Home Depot,  count 1
nodes on some closable loop    2 of 20
nodes on no loop at all       18 of 20
```

**Eighteen of twenty nodes sit on no cycle.** By Theorem 1 their price relations
are not falsifiable inside this graph, and that is Menger's count with one route at
its most extreme, on published data.

**Maximum hop distance to Amazon: one.** Best Buy, Home Depot and Lowe's are each
one hop; every other node is unreachable.

## The self edge, which is a separate published decision

Whether a seller reconciles its **own two channels** is published too, it varies,
and it is independent of the external out-degree.

| seller | self edge | published wording |
|---|---|---|
| Best Buy | **closes** | matches online and app prices on in-store purchases, and in-store prices on online and app purchases |
| Home Depot | **closes** | matches homedepot.com, with a 30-day adjustment there |
| Target | **closes** | since 2025-07-28 this is the only edge it keeps: *"only match prices for items sold in-store at Target or on Target.com"* |
| Lowe's | **refuses** | excludes *"Prices from one Lowe's store location to another"* |
| Costco | **refuses** | *"Costco does not price match Costco warehouse prices for Costco.com purchases. Costco.com prices take into account shipping and handling fees not applicable to warehouse purchases."* |
| Walmart | **refuses** | excludes both *"Items purchased from Walmart.com that later decrease in price"* and *"Items offered in Walmart stores"* |
| Amazon | **refuses** | no policy at all |

**Three close, four refuse, every one of them in writing.** The combinations are
crossed: Costco and Walmart have out-degree zero **and** refuse the self edge;
Target has out-degree zero and keeps **only** the self edge; Best Buy has
out-degree nineteen and closes it.

**Costco's refusal states the mechanism this project has been arguing for.** It
declines to reconcile its own two prices for its own object **on the ground that
the two channels are not selling the same thing**, because one price embeds
shipping and handling and the other does not. **That is a substitution-class
assertion, made by the seller, in writing, about itself.** It is the same object
as the web-versus-mobile subscription gap recorded above, with the reasoning made
explicit instead of left to be inferred.

**Lowe's refusal is the sharper one.** It excludes matching **between its own
stores**. One brand, one item, two branches, and the company declines in writing to
align the two numbers.

## How the sinks were made, with dates

| year | party | what was withdrawn |
|---|---|---|
| 2016 | Amazon | post-purchase price adjustments against other retailers, televisions excepted |
| 2017 | Walmart | matching competitors at checkout |
| 2019 | Walmart | Savings Catcher, the automatic post-purchase comparison |
| 2025-07-28 | Target | competitor matching, keeping only its own two channels |

**These three parties are exactly the top in-degree nodes in the graph today.**
They became anchors by **ceasing to watch while continuing to be watched**, over
nine years. **An anchor here is not found. It is made, and the making is dated.**

By Theorem 7 a thinner observation graph carries more surviving inconsistency, so
the direction of this sequence is toward more of it, not less.

## The stated reasons have one shape, and it is self sealing

> **Walmart, on ending Savings Catcher:** *"Walmart's prices win most often when
> you submit your receipts, which tells us that the program's intent has been met."*

> **Target, 2025:** *"We've found our guests overwhelmingly price match Target and
> not other retailers, which reflects the great value and trust in pricing
> consumers see across our assortment and deals."*

**Both parties cite the check's own past results as the reason the check is no
longer needed. Once the edge is gone that claim cannot be retested**, because the
edge was the only thing that could have falsified it.

**Target's has a second turn in it.** It reports that customers mostly compare
Target against Target. **Customers can only compare what the seller permits**, so
the observation offered as the reason for removing the external edge is also a
consequence of how narrow the edge already was.

**This is not a claim that either party is insincere.** Both statements may be
true. The point is structural: **after the removal, neither is checkable, and the
removal is what made them uncheckable.**

## What this chunk kills

**B30-1 cannot run on this carrier.** Its discriminating cell is a node with **no
direct access to the source and a long relay path**. Maximum hop distance here is
one: everybody who watches at all watches Amazon directly. **There is no hop
gradient, so there is nothing to measure decay against.**

**B30-5 cannot run on this carrier either.** The graph gives structure and not
timing, and the front needs a propagation history.

**Finding this out cost one chunk of graph building and no data collection at
all**, which is the entire reason the graph was built before either reading.

## What it establishes

1. **The anchor is readable off the published observation graph** with no price
   data: highest in-degree, zero out-degree.
2. **Anchors are made, and the making is dated.** Three withdrawals over nine
   years, each by a party that is now a top in-degree node.
3. **Checkability is almost absent.** One closable loop among twenty nodes;
   eighteen nodes on none.
4. **The self edge is a separate, published, varying decision**, and two sellers
   decline to reconcile their own prices in writing, one of them stating the
   substitution-class reason outright.
5. **Sellers disagree in writing about who is a competitor.** Best Buy names the
   membership clubs as qualified; Home Depot and Lowe's exclude them by name.
   **The substitution class is asserted, and the assertions conflict.**

## Limits

1. **Thirteen out-edge lists uncollected.** They are all currently zero-out-degree
   sinks by assumption, and each one collected could add edges and cycles.
   **The count of one closable loop is a lower bound.**
2. **The pre-withdrawal edge lists are not recovered.** The sources record that
   matching ended and when, not whom each party had named. **So no before-and-after
   cycle count can be computed**, and none is offered.
3. **Home Depot's page is an archive supplied by hand**, dated to
   2025-08-22, while the captured HTML carries an embedded rewrite stamp of
   2025-12-07. **The two are not reconciled.** The live page did not serve the
   policy body on 2026-08-30.
4. **Walmart's and Costco's policy texts, and Amazon's sentence, were supplied by
   hand rather than fetched here.** They are treated as official because they
   are the parties' own wording, and the provenance is recorded.
5. **Walmart's current policy is dated 2023-06-07 while its withdrawal was 2017.**
   The six years of intervening policy text are not examined.
---

# B30-6, chunk one: the restriction is a per-line decision and it is published

**Read 2026-08-30.** The arm asks whether channel restriction tracks the
cross-market price gap. **This chunk establishes that the restriction exists, is
discretionary, and is applied to some product lines and not others, all from the
maker's own warranty documents.** It does not yet test the arm.

## The two documents

**The iOS warranty document, current since 2024-01-19, verbatim:**

> *"Apple **may restrict** warranty service for iPhone, iPad, Apple TV, HomePod,
> and Apple Vision Pro to the country where Apple or its Authorized Distributors
> originally sold the device."*

**The Mac warranty document, current since 2022-04-22, has no such clause.** Its
geographic language is the opposite in effect:

> *"This Warranty is governed by and construed under the laws of the country in
> which the Apple Product purchase took place."*
> *"Service will be limited to the options available in the country where service
> is requested. Service options, parts availability and response times may vary
> according to country."*

**Coverage travels; only the service options vary.**

## Three things this settles

**One. The restriction is a reserved discretion, not a rule.** *"May restrict"*
names a power and does not say where it is used. **So the restriction's extent is
an exercised policy rather than a published one**, and measuring it means finding
where it bites, not reading a list.

**Two. It is applied line by line, by the same maker, in the same jurisdiction, in
writing.** iPhone, iPad, Apple TV, HomePod and Vision Pro are named. Mac is not
subject to it at all. **This is the substitution-and-edge machinery of §12.9 and
§12.1 appearing as a warranty clause: the maker decides, per line, whether a unit
bought in one market is usable in another.**

**Three, and this is the analytical result of the chunk: the obvious competing
explanation is already weakened by the list itself.** The natural alternative is
that the restriction tracks **regional hardware and regulatory variance**, since
cellular bands and radio certification differ by country. **The named list does not
fit that.**

- **Wi-Fi-only iPad has no cellular hardware.** Apple TV and HomePod have none
  either. All three are restricted.
- **Mac has Wi-Fi and Bluetooth radios requiring the same class of certification**
  as those three, and is not restricted.

**So on the radio-certification variable the restricted and unrestricted sets are
not separated.** A regulatory account has to explain why a Wi-Fi iPad is restricted
and a MacBook is not, and neither document offers one. **This does not establish
the price-gap account; it removes the easiest rival from the table.**

## What the arm still needs

1. **Cross-market price dispersion per line.** [`b25`](b25_price_band_availability.md)
   has it for one handset, 41 countries, range **2.05**, absolute gap about
   **$1,119** between the dearest and cheapest. **Nothing comparable is collected
   for Mac, AirPods or Apple Watch**, and without it the central comparison cannot
   be made.
2. **Where the discretion is actually exercised.** Since the clause is permissive,
   the measurement is by country. Two leads, neither yet pulled: **trade reporting
   that one large market's arm began providing international warranty on the
   handset**, which would be a dated relaxation, and **user reports that another
   market declines to honour service on units bought elsewhere**. Both need
   primary confirmation and neither is used here.
3. **A matched pair on price gap.** The cleanest test available is **Wi-Fi iPad
   against MacBook**: both radio-certified, both portable, one restricted and one
   not. **If the iPad's cross-market gap is larger, the arm's first branch fires on
   a pair the regulatory account cannot separate.**

## What would still kill it

**A restricted line with a small cross-market gap, or an unrestricted line with a
large one.** Mac is the candidate for the second, and its dispersion is exactly the
number not yet collected. **The chunk is deliberately stopping here rather than
asserting a direction on one line's data.**

---

# B30-6, chunk two: the restriction does not track the price gap, and the first branch fails

**The pair the previous chunk asked for has been collected, and it answers against
the arm.**

## The instrument

A dedicated comparison site tracking **the maker's official prices across 39
countries and 25 products**, indexed to the United States, **shown before and
after tax with tourist refunds deducted where available**, converted at Open
Exchange Rates. **One method across all lines**, which is what makes the
cross-line comparison possible at all. **Third-party tier.**

## The reading

| product | warranty restricted | cheapest | dearest | ratio | absolute gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| iPhone 17 Pro 256GB | **yes** | Hong Kong $1,198 | Brazil $2,213 | **1.847** | $1,015 |
| MacBook Neo 256GB | **no** | Hong Kong $701 | Brazil $1,636 | **2.334** | $935 |
| Mac mini M5 Pro | **no** | Japan $1,872 | UK $2,300 | 1.229 | $428 |

```
by ratio          MacBook 2.334  >  iPhone 1.847  >  Mac mini 1.229
by absolute gap   iPhone 1,015   >  MacBook 935   >  Mac mini 428
```

**The arm's first branch requires the restricted line to carry the larger gap. On
the ratio it does not: the unrestricted MacBook is the widest of the three. On the
absolute gap the restricted iPhone leads by 8.6 per cent, which is not a separation
on three points.**

**So the first branch fails.** The restriction does not track the cross-market
price gap on the only cross-line comparison available.

## What is not thereby established

**The second branch, that the restriction tracks logistics or regulation, is not
established either**, and the previous chunk already showed why it cannot be:
Wi-Fi iPad, Apple TV and HomePod carry no cellular hardware and are restricted,
while a MacBook with the same class of radio certification is not. **Both
registered branches are now unsupported on this carrier**, which is the arm's
third state: **undecided, with the framework's own branch specifically knocked
out.**

## Three reasons this reading is weaker than it looks, all against my own result

1. **Three products is not a panel.** Two of them are Macs and they sit at opposite
   ends of the ratio ordering, 2.334 and 1.229. **A variable that puts two members
   of the unrestricted class at both extremes is not being measured by three
   points.**
2. **Brazil drives both wide ranges and is a known tax outlier.**
   [`b25`](b25_price_band_availability.md) found that excluding Türkiye and Brazil
   took a handset range from **2.05 to 1.43**, a third of the spread coming from
   two tax regimes. **Both wide ranges here have Brazil at the top**, so the
   comparison as it stands is partly a comparison of how two tax regimes treat two
   product categories. **The ex-outlier comparison needs the full country tables
   and they were not pulled.**
3. **The products are not matched.** A $699 laptop and a $1,099 handset differ in
   category, in tariff classification and in how import duty is assessed. **The
   matched pair the previous chunk asked for was Wi-Fi iPad against MacBook**, and
   the iPad figure is still not collected.

## Where this leaves the arm

**The framework's prediction on this arm is not supported and is reported as not
supported.** That is the second negative from this station today, after B28-6, and
the two are unrelated, which is the useful part: **the criteria are returning
different answers on different arms rather than agreeing with the file that wrote
them.**

**What would revive it, and it is a real possibility rather than a face-saving
one.** The restriction may track something other than the gap, and the obvious
candidate is **how easily a unit crosses a border in a suitcase and is resold**,
for which the named list is a better fit than either registered branch: phones,
tablets, a streaming puck, a speaker and a headset are all carry-on items with
resale markets, and a desktop Mac mini is not. **But a MacBook is**, and it is
unrestricted, so even that account has to explain the same counterexample.
**Nothing here is claimed for it; it is recorded as the next hypothesis and it is
not this arm's registered one.**

---

# B30-6, chunk three: a six-product panel, complete interleaving, and a statistic that turns out to be the wrong one

**The previous chunk read three products and said three was not a panel. Six were
collected from the same instrument.** Restriction status is taken from the two
warranty documents: iPhone, iPad and Apple TV are named in the iOS document; the
Macs are covered by a document with no country-of-purchase clause.

| rank | product | restricted | ratio | cheapest | dearest |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | iPad Wi-Fi 128GB | **yes** | **3.465** | Japan $333 | Brazil $1,154 |
| 2 | MacBook Air 13 M5 | no | 2.433 | Thailand $1,266 | Brazil $3,080 |
| 3 | MacBook Neo 256GB | no | 2.334 | Hong Kong $701 | Brazil $1,636 |
| 4 | Apple TV 4K 128GB | **yes** | 1.994 | Thailand $169 | UK $337 |
| 5 | iPhone 17 Pro 256GB | **yes** | 1.847 | Hong Kong $1,198 | Brazil $2,213 |
| 6 | Mac mini M5 Pro | no | 1.229 | Japan $1,872 | UK $2,300 |

```
restricted     median 1.994   mean 2.436   values 3.465, 1.994, 1.847
unrestricted   median 2.334   mean 1.998   values 2.433, 2.334, 1.229
rank sums      restricted 10, unrestricted 11
               complete separation on three against three would be 6 against 15
```

**The two classes interleave completely.** The restricted set holds ranks 1, 4 and
5; the unrestricted set holds 2, 3 and 6. **The rank sums differ by one.** On the
median the unrestricted set is the wider of the two.

**The first branch fails on a panel, not on three points.** The restriction does
not track the cross-market price gap.

## And the statistic itself does not survive the collection

**The dearest country is Brazil for four products and the United Kingdom for two.**
Sorting the panel by whether Brazil appears sorts it almost perfectly by ratio:

```
Brazil in the sample        3.465, 2.433, 2.334, 1.847
Brazil not in the sample                    1.994, 1.229
```

**Three of the four widest are Brazil-topped and both narrow ones are not.** So the
ratio of extremes is substantially measuring **whether the highest-tax market
carries that product at all**, which is a fact about distribution and tax
schedules rather than about the maker's channel policy.

**This is the same defect [`b25`](b25_price_band_availability.md) already
documented on its own panel**, where dropping two tax regimes took a range from
2.05 to 1.43. **The right statistic is dispersion over a country set held common
across products**, and the per-product pages give only the two extremes, so it
cannot be computed from what was collected.

**So the arm returns two things and they should not be conflated.** The registered
first branch fails on the evidence available. **And the evidence available is
partly measuring something else**, which means the failure is reported at the
strength it has: **a clean interleaving on a poor statistic, not a clean refutation
on a good one.**

## What a proper run needs

1. **Full country tables per product**, not the two extremes, so dispersion can be
   taken over a fixed country set.
2. **The remaining restricted lines**, HomePod and the iPad variants, and the
   remaining Macs, to take the panel past three against three.
3. **The warranty status of Watch, AirPods and AirTag**, whose documents have not
   been read. They were kept out of the panel for that reason rather than assumed
   unrestricted.

**Until those exist the arm stands as reported: the framework's prediction is not
supported, and the instrument that failed to support it is itself not good enough
to have supported it.**

---

# B30-12, chunk one: three cases where the posted number sits below what anyone pays

**Read 2026-08-30**, against the criteria registered for this arm.
**This chunk fills the upward cell only.** The downward cell already had three
cases in this file; the upward cell had one, and a single point was not enough to
carry a category.

**The category, from §1b.** A posted number can do three jobs: be the level others
price against (**anchor**), be what changes hands (**transacted price**), or **be
the number the poster can point to when challenged (a stated position)**. The third
has four signatures, all observable, and a fifth that is the strongest of all.

## Case one: a graphics card

| observation | value | over the posted $1,999 |
|---|---|---|
| US median street, 2026-08 | $4,700 | **2.35x** |
| Amazon spot, 07-11 | $4,329 | 2.17x |
| a partner card at a large retailer, 07-30 | $4,830 | 2.42x |
| South Korea, 08 | $5,112 | 2.56x |

**And the maker itself raised the price without raising its own number.** On
2026-05-13 it notified board partners of a further estimated **$300** increase;
one partner raised first and two followed. **The $1,999 did not move.**

## Case two: a minivan in China, where the maker states the mechanism itself

Manufacturer's suggested price **899,000 yuan** for the named trim.

| dealer | markup | transacted | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hangzhou, new model | 160,000 | 1,059,000 | **1.178** |
| Hangzhou, previous model | 100,000 | 999,000 | 1.111 |
| prevailing market | 100,000 | 999,000 | 1.111 |
| parallel import, previous model | 40,000 | 939,000 | 1.044 |
| one quoted offer | 27,500 | 926,500 | 1.031 |

**No stock at the posted price in five provinces**: Beijing, Shanghai, Guangzhou,
Fujian and Zhejiang all reported none. **One Fujian dealer's annual allocation was
two to three cars and was already sold out.**

**And the maker's own explanation of why the transacted price is not its
responsibility, verbatim:**

> *"从国家法律规定来讲，总经销商（丰田中国）是没有权利和资格去要求经销商对车型定价的。
> 厂家会有建议零售价，但不能去约束经销商。"*

**That is the deniability sentence spoken by the party, with a legal ground under
it.** The posted number is kept, the transaction is attributed to the dealer, and
the attribution is presented as a matter of law rather than of choice.

**This is a sharper instance than the graphics card**, because there the routing
has to be inferred from the partner notification and here it is asserted as a
defence.

## Case three: tickets, and this one reaches the fifth signature

**The strongest cell is when the poster earns from the secondary market**, because
then the relation between "nobody can buy at the posted price" and "the poster's
revenue" is positive rather than negative, and both the scarcity account and the
benign account fail at once.

A 2018 CBC and Toronto Star investigation documented an invitation-only platform
through which approved resellers buy in bulk and reprice by perceived demand;
about **100 North American resellers** used it, the largest handling roughly
**five million tickets a year**; and **the operator takes a percentage of both
legs**, the sale to the reseller and the resale to the consumer. Staff on the
record: *"I have brokers that have literally a couple of hundred accounts"* and
*"It's not something that we look at or report."* The company was at the same time
publicly litigating against scalping.

## The four signatures, scored

| signature | card | minivan | tickets |
|---|---|---|---|
| transacted price persistently diverges | yes, 2.35x | yes, 1.03 to 1.18x | yes |
| poster holds its own number | yes | yes | yes |
| volume at the number is small and poster-controlled | partly, allocation | **yes, no stock, 2 to 3 cars a year** | yes, held back inventory |
| an intermediary posts the real number | yes | **yes, and the maker cites law for it** | yes |
| **poster earns from the secondary market** | no | no | **yes** |

## What this chunk establishes and does not

**Establishes.** The upward cell has three independent cases across three
industries, and the four signatures fire in all three. **The category is no longer
carried by one point.** And one case reaches the fifth signature, which is the one
that excludes the benign readings.

**Does not establish** that the upward pattern is common rather than confined to
allocation-constrained goods. **All three are supply-limited items**, which is the
competing explanation named in the design and not yet excluded.

**The discriminating observation, still not collected.** A firm facing scarcity and
wanting the rent raises **its own** price. Two of these three passed the increase
downstream instead. **The test is whether other firms in the same shortage chose to
raise their own number**, and that comparison has not been made.

## Limits

1. **Ratios are not comparable across the three.** A 2.35x on a card and a 1.18x on
   a car are different objects: one is a scalping premium on a mass item, one is a
   dealer markup on an allocated import. **They are three instances of a pattern,
   not three measurements of one quantity.**
2. **The ticket case is 2018** and the platform's terms may have changed. It is
   used for the fifth signature, which is structural, not for a current price.
3. **The minivan figures are trade reporting of dealer quotes**, not transaction
   records, and dealer quotes are the ask rather than the settled price.

---

# B30-12, chunk two: the discriminating observation, and then the mechanism running in reverse on a date

## Part one: scarcity does not require holding your own number

**The competing explanation named in chunk one was real scarcity.** The
discriminating observation was registered as: **in the same shortage, did any firm
raise its own posted number instead of passing the increase downstream?**

**It did.** On **2021-05-31**, in the same industry-wide chip and materials
shortage, a direct-selling carmaker's chief executive said of its own price
increases:

> *"Prices increasing due to major supply chain price pressure industry-wide. Raw
> materials especially."*

and that was **the fifth incremental increase in a few months** on its two volume
models. **That firm has no dealer layer.**

**Against the minivan case in the same shortage**: the guide price of 899,000 yuan
did not move, dealers added 100,000 to 160,000, and the maker stated it had no
authority over dealer pricing.

```
no dealer layer      the firm's own number moved five times, with a public reason
dealer layer         the firm's own number did not move; the gap was taken and
                     defended by the intermediary
```

**So holding the number is not what scarcity forces. It is what an intermediary
makes possible.** Scarcity is present in both and the handling differs by
structure.

**One asymmetry to state before it is used against this.** The direct case is one
jurisdiction and the intermediated case is another, and the law the minivan maker
cited is specific to the second. **The comparison is therefore suggestive across
jurisdictions and needs a within-jurisdiction version**, which is part two.

## Part two: the same jurisdiction, the same brands, and the structural variable changed on a date

**Three European premium marques moved their new electric lines in China from a
dealer-negotiated price to a single national price.** Reported 2026-04-19.

**Before:** the stated model was **"高指导价、大额终端优惠"**, a high guide price
with large terminal discounts. **Those discounts ran from 80,000 to nearly 200,000
yuan.**

**After:** **"一口价"**, and the phrase used is **"指导价即售价"**, the guide price
is the selling price. One marque describes the arrangement as **"融合直售"**, with
customers ordering **"通过官方统一平台下单，全国实行统一价格"**.

**And the conversion is stated arithmetically by the reporting.** For one saloon,
**"其官方指导价较老款下调超过10万元，将终端优惠转为官降"**: the official guide
price was cut by more than 100,000 yuan against the previous model, **converting
the terminal discount into an official price cut.**

A salesperson on the new electric models: **"官方定价不高，因此几乎没有议价空间"**,
and future models will **"统一沿用这一透明定价模式"**.

## Why this is the strongest single item this station has

**It is the mechanism run in reverse, deliberately, by the party, on a date, inside
one jurisdiction and one brand.**

```
intermediary present   posted number high and held; the real price is posted by
                       the dealer; the gap is 80,000 to 200,000 yuan
intermediary removed   the posted number must become the price, and it falls by
                       roughly the gap it used to hide
```

**The transacted price is not what changed. The posted number is.** That is the
clearest available demonstration that the two are different objects: **remove the
party that was posting the real number, and the nominal number has to come down to
meet it.**

**And it answers the scarcity objection without needing the cross-jurisdiction
pair.** Nothing about supply changed on the date the pricing mode changed. **The
variable that moved was whether an intermediary stood between the poster and the
buyer**, and the posted number moved with it.

## What is established and what is inferred

**Established from the source**: the mode change and its date; the pre-change
discount range of 80,000 to nearly 200,000 yuan; the phrase 指导价即售价; the
unified national platform; the saloon's guide price cut of more than 100,000 yuan
described as converting terminal discount into official cut; and both quoted
remarks.

**Inferred, and marked as inferred**: that the **transacted** price was
approximately unchanged across the switch. The reporting's own phrase implies it,
but no before-and-after transaction price is given. **A proper reading needs paired
transacted prices across the switch date**, and that is the next collection.

## Limits

1. **One trade report.** The mode change is corroborated across several outlets in
   the same week, but the discount range and the saloon's figure come from this one.
2. **The switch is confined to new electric lines**, so it is not a clean before
   and after on one model. The saloon comparison is against **the previous
   generation**, which changes the object as well as the pricing mode.
3. **Direction of causation is not established.** These brands were losing share in
   China and the switch may be a response to that rather than to the pricing
   structure itself. **What the case establishes is that the posted number tracks
   the presence of an intermediary, not why the intermediary was removed.**

---

---

# B30-12, chunk three: the paired reading across the switch, and the correction it forces

**Read 2026-08-30.** Chunk two closed by marking one thing as inferred rather than
established: that the **transacted** price was approximately unchanged across the
BBA move to 一口价. This chunk collects the paired numbers. **The inference holds
in one half and fails in the other, and the failure sharpens the mechanism instead
of breaking it.**

## The one carrier where both legs exist

一汽奥迪A6L. The new generation launched **2026-03-25** at **32.29万-43.59万元**,
with 搜狐汽车 recording that 起步价相比上一代车型降了近10万元.

| | posted 官方指导价 | transacted 裸车价, June 2026 |
|---|---|---|
| **old 2025款 40 TFSI豪华动感型** | **42.79万** | **23.99万-29.18万**, 最高优惠18.8万 |
| **new 2026款 臻选型** | **32.29万** | **29.29万-30.09万** 华北/中原, 现金优惠2.2万-3万<br>**30.79万-31.59万** 华东/华南, 现金优惠0.7万-1.5万 |

Guide-price cut: 42.79 minus 32.29 = **10.50万**, which matches the 官降10.49万
figure and the 北京商报 line quoted in chunk two, 其官方指导价较老款下调超过10万元,
将终端优惠转为官降.

**Three things fall out of this table and only the first was predicted.**

**1. The cut is the right size.** The maker's cut is the same order as the discount
the car was already selling at. That half of chunk two's inference stands.

**2. The terminal discount did not go to zero. It reopened within three months.**
The new car carries 现金优惠 of 0.7万 to 3.0万 in June 2026, on the model the trade
press listed as inside the 一口价 system. **一口价 is not self-enforcing on a
carrier that keeps its dealer layer.**

**3. The decreed 全国统一价格 carries about 1.5万 of regional dispersion in the
transacted number, three months after launch.** 华北 and 中原 discount 2.2万 to
3.0万; 华东 and 华南 discount 0.7万 to 1.5万. **One posted number by decree, and the
residual sits on region.** That is Theorem 7's reading at its plainest: making the
posted number uniform does not make the field the gradient of anything, it only
moves where the disagreement is carried.

## The comparison that is not clean, stated before anything is built on it

**The old model's June-2026 transacted price is a run-out.** Its 18.8万 discount is
a liquidation number against a successor already on sale, not the steady-state
pre-switch discount. **So "transacted price approximately unchanged" cannot be read
off this pair directly**, and it is not claimed here.

**The pre-launch transacted price of the old A6L was searched for and not obtained
at a usable tier.** One trade-press lead exists, 网通社 reporting 降16.59万 to
裸车28.20万起, and **the domain redirect-loops between http and https and could not
be fetched**. It is recorded as an open lead, not used.

## The carrier where the switch did hold, and what is different about it

奔驰纯电CLA, launched 2025-11 under 一口价.

> **北京商报, 2026-06-16**, a dealer salesperson: *"纯电CLA采用的是'一口价'模式，
> 上市至今也没有降价"*

**Eight months, no discount.** And in the same showroom, in the same month, the
lines **not** on 一口价:

| model | posted | dealer discount |
|---|---|---|
| **EQE500** | 47.89万 | **21万-22万**, that is 44 to 46 per cent of the posted number |
| **C260L** | from 29.99万 | up to 10万; one sold at **25万** |
| **AMG EQS** | | *"降价120万元后才售出"* |
| **纯电CLA** | from 24.9万 | **none** |

The salesperson states the boundary himself, and it is not a boundary between
objects:

> *"更多是宣传作用，纯电GLC价格现在全国统一，油车价格可以再谈。"*

**So the discriminating variable is not whether the maker announced 一口价. It is
whether the intermediary retains discretion over the final number.** 一汽奥迪 kept
its dealer network on the traditional footing and the gap regenerated in three
months. Mercedes's new electric line removed dealer discretion outright, with a
dealer confirming 出厂政策调整后无议价空间, and the number held.

**This is a correction to chunk two.** Chunk two read the switch as the mechanism
run in reverse by the party on a date. It is, but **the party can only run it where
it also removes the discretion.** Announcing a uniform price while leaving a
discounting layer in place produces a smaller gap, not no gap.

## Where the adjustment went on the carrier that held

This is the part that matters most, because it is the chain's own margin claim at
the limit. With the price fixed and no dealer able to absorb anything, the
adjustment on 纯电CLA ran through two channels, and neither is price negotiation.

**Quantity.** Monthly sales, 上市首月销量为1369辆, then 2月仅售出21辆，3月回升至358辆，
4月又跌至52辆, with 上市半年多的累计销量仅1800辆.

**The maker moving its own posted number.** At the 北京车展 in late April 2026,
奔驰又进一步推出了售价22.9万元的CLA260L入门版, against an opening floor of 24.9万.

**So removing the intermediary does not remove the adjustment. It relocates it, to
quantity first and to the maker's own posted number second.** That is
[`b26`](b26_results.md) and [`b28`](b28_results.md)'s finding with the dealer layer
deleted, and it is the cleanest instance the chain has of it.

## The distribution edge that bought nothing

Registered under §1a of the design file as a side reading, and it came out clean.

Mercedes sold 纯电GLC through 李佳琦's live stream, the largest single distribution
and information edge in the Chinese consumer market. Several dealers told 北京商报
the same thing:

> *"在直播间下定与在门店下定，奔驰方面所能提供的优惠一致。"*

What the channel got instead was a side payment that is not the price: a 3000元
deposit 可享3000元订金膨胀至1万元的优惠, plus a 5000元天猫实物红包 on delivery.

**The largest distribution edge in the country could not move the posted number by
one yuan. It could attach a coupon.** That is B28's non-price margin appearing on
the buyer's side of the same object, and it is a direct answer to the question this
station opened with about what an information or distribution edge buys. **On this
carrier it does not buy price.**

## The within-brand structural pair, which is the second one this station has found

奥迪 runs **both modes at once through its two joint ventures**: same badge, same
country, same month (21世纪经济报道, 2026-07-18, 郑植文).

| joint venture | mode | examples |
|---|---|---|
| **上汽奥迪** | the maker posts the discount itself, 权益价 / 官方限时价 | A7L, guide 41.87万-66.62万, to **29.9万起**<br>Q6, guide 46.76万-61.06万, to **29.9万起**<br>A5L Sportback, guide 27.99万-39.99万, to **25.99万起** |
| **一汽奥迪** | dealer discounts against a high guide price | A6L 2026款40TFSI 裸车低至**25.8万**<br>Q5L 2025款40TFSI 裸车约**24万** against guide 36.98万 |

Salesperson, same report: *"部分车型终端优惠幅度已经达到15万至16万元"*.

**One brand, two intermediary structures, two entirely different relationships
between the posted number and the transacted one, with nothing about the object
differing.** This is the cleanest available demonstration that **the posted-to-
transacted gap is a property of the channel and not of the good.**

## What this chunk establishes

1. **The guide-price cut on the switch is the same order as the discount the car
   was already selling at**, 10.49万 against pre-existing discounts in the same
   region.
2. **一口价 does not hold where the dealer layer keeps discretion.** Discounts of
   0.7万 to 3.0万 reappeared on the new A6L within three months of launch.
3. **A decreed national uniform price still carries about 1.5万 of regional
   dispersion in the transacted number** three months in.
4. **Where dealer discretion is genuinely removed the number holds**, eight months
   and counting, **and the adjustment relocates to quantity and then to the maker's
   own posted number.**
5. **Within one brand at one date, lines with and without an intermediary show a
   zero and a 44 to 46 per cent posted-to-transacted gap.**
6. **The largest distribution edge in the market bought no price concession**, only
   a non-price side payment.

## What it does not establish

1. **The pre-switch steady-state transacted price of the old A6L.** Not obtained at
   a usable tier, and the June-2026 figures for that car are run-out liquidation.
2. **Direction of causation on the mode change.** The makers switched while under
   competitive pressure from domestic electric makers; the mode change and the
   price cut are simultaneous and this reading does not separate them.
3. **Whether the CLA's hold survives.** The April addition of a cheaper trim is
   already the posted number moving, by a different route.
4. **Anything about buyers.** No reading here requires anyone to be uninformed.

## Limits

1. **The A6L transacted figures come from one 汽车之家 forum thread.** First-hand
   and regionally resolved, which is why it is used, but one thread.
2. **Several items in the same result set are unusable and none of the figures
   above come from them.** One is labelled 本文由AI生成 outright; another gives
   2025款A6L入门版指导价 as 32.29万, which is the **new** model's price, an internal
   contradiction. **They are recorded here so a later reader does not re-find them
   and treat them as corroboration.**
3. **网通社 (auto.news18a.com) redirect-loops and could not be fetched.** Two leads
   sit behind it.
4. **The CLA guide range is reported as 24.9万-29.99万 by one outlet and
   24.9万-28.56万 by another. Not reconciled.** The floor, 24.9万, is consistent
   across every source and **only the floor is used above.**
5. **The 上汽奥迪 权益价 figures are posted promotional prices, not observed
   transactions.** They establish that the maker posts the cut itself, which is what
   the table uses them for, and nothing about what was paid.
