# B30-23, chunk one: the availability pass, and the arm does not die on it

**Read 2026-09-03** against the design file's section 4. **No filing was pulled and
no case has been named.** This block establishes only whether the criterion's
object can be retrieved at all, which is the question that decides whether the arm
is worth naming a case for.

## What the criterion needs, and it is unusual

The criterion counts **distinct class values in a filed rating plan**. That is not
a price series and not an estimate. **It is a property of a document**, so the
availability question is not how deep a sample is but whether the document itself
is public and whether its attachments come with it. A filing index that carries
only the approved percentage change is useless here; the rating plan exhibit is
the whole object.

## The reading

**Rate filings in the United States move through one system, and several states
publish it.** Confirmed open by direct address: **Michigan, California, Texas,
Washington, Indiana**. The exhaustive participating list sits behind a rendered
page and was not enumerated, and it does not need to be: **five large states is
already more than the criterion requires, which is one state where both parties
filed.**

**Michigan states the scope in its own words**: all rate, form and rule filings are
available for inspecting and downloading at no cost. **And the attachments have
been shown to come through by a third party who did it**: a 2024 investigation
pulled private passenger auto filings for seven companies covering 83 per cent of
that market and extracted the rate calculation table together with the supporting
documentation of what inputs each factor uses. **That is exactly the object this
criterion counts, retrieved by someone else, at no cost, and reported in a
published method note.**

> **The arm does not die on availability. The retrieval condition is satisfied.**

## What the binding number is now

It is no longer the number of open states. **It is the number of transfers where
both parties filed a personal-lines rating plan in an open state around a dated
change of control, and where the design file's section 3 pre-check holds, that the
two counts differed before the transfer.** That census has not been built.

## One route walked and recorded as not obviously available

**A liquidation is not a transfer of the kind this criterion needs.** When an
insurer is placed in liquidation its policies go to the guaranty association,
which pays claims; the policyholders are not moved as a block onto another
carrier's rating plan. **Only an assumption agreement does that**, and whether
those are enumerable with both parties' filings retrievable was not established
here. **Recorded so the next pass does not walk it again as though it were
obvious.** The cross-border route is untouched and is the one the design file
ranks first.

## Limits

1. **Nothing was pulled.** No filing, no case, no count.
2. **The open-state list is a floor and not a census.** Five confirmed by address;
   the full list was not enumerated and the arm does not need it.
3. **Whether older filings are retained online was not checked**, and it bites
   directly: the criterion needs the filing before the transfer, which may predate
   what a state keeps posted. **That is the next question and it is cheap.**

---

---

# B30-23, chunk two: how far back the filings go, and why the question is not about retention

**Read 2026-09-03**, settling chunk one's third limit. **The criterion needs the
filing from before a transfer, so the window matters more than the breadth.**

## The question is structural, not a policy

**A rate filing made before a state moved to electronic filing is not in the
system at all.** It is paper. So the floor is not a retention period that could be
looked up and applied uniformly; **it is the date each state moved**, and it
differs by state. Two state pages were read and **neither states a window**: the
Michigan search page gives no earliest date and no retention note, and the
California page names the lines covered and says nothing about dates.

## What is established, and it is one demonstrated datum rather than a policy

**Michigan holds filings from at least 2018.** The 2024 investigation that pulled
that state's private passenger auto filings **compared filings from before and
after the 2019 reform for two carriers**, which cannot be done unless the
pre-reform filings were retrievable. **That is a demonstration and not a claim
about the schedule.**

> **The gate clears on the evidence available, and the exact window is unread.
> This is the middle state, not a pass and not a failure.**

## Two things named rather than assumed

1. **A document exists that would settle it**: the retention programme page on the
   filing system's own site. **It could not be retrieved from this session**, and
   that is an instrument limit and not a finding. Anyone with ordinary access can
   open it.
2. **A commercial route exists and was not enumerated in chunk one**: a vendor
   sells a systematically collected dataset of insurance product filings. **It
   costs money and it is named here only so the source classes are complete.**

## What this does to the arm

**Transfers from roughly 2019 onward are inside the demonstrated window in at
least one large state.** Older ones are not excluded, they are unverified.
**So the census chunk one asked for should be built forward from the recent end
rather than backward from the interesting end**, because the recent end is where
retrievability is demonstrated rather than assumed.

## Limits

1. **One state, one demonstration.** Michigan at 2018 is a floor for Michigan and
   says nothing about the other four.
2. **Nothing was pulled here either.** No filing, no case, no count.

---

---

# B30-23, chunk three: the census, built forward from the demonstrated end

**Read 2026-09-03**, answering the binding number chunk one left. **Still nothing
pulled.** This block names cases and marks, per case, what is verified and what is
not. Built from the recent end, as chunk two's reading requires, because that is
where retrievability is demonstrated rather than assumed.

## The first case, and it clears every condition the design file names

| | |
|---|---|
| **event** | the Farmers Exchanges and Farmers Group acquire MetLife Auto & Home's property and casualty business |
| **announced** | **2020-12-11** |
| **closed** | **2021-04-07** |
| **consideration** | 3.94 billion dollars |
| **lines** | **personal auto and homeowners** |
| **cross-border** | **yes**, the ultimate acquirer is a Swiss group and Farmers Group is its United States management company |

**Every condition in the design file's section 4 is met.** The channel is the one
that section ranks first. The lines are the ones where a rating plan carries the
most structure. The date sits inside the window chunk two demonstrated. And the
world is held fixed with unusual force: **the policyholders, their cars, their
houses, their states, their regulators and their claims environment are the same
on 8 April as on 6 April.**

**One feature was not anticipated when the criterion was written and it helps.**
The acquired business carried a large employer-sponsored group channel, so **the
two parties' rating plans differed in structure and not only in their numbers**.
Section 3's pre-check, that the two counts differ before the transfer, is
therefore very likely to hold here. **Likely is not measured**, and it is the
first thing to read.

## Two more, ranked below it and marked for what is unverified

| case | channel | status |
|---|---|---|
| a Japanese group acquires a high-net-worth personal lines writer, 2020 | cross-border, the first-ranked channel | **dates not verified here.** Named so the census is not a census of one |
| a large domestic mutual acquires a mid-sized personal lines group, closing 2022 | ordinary merger, the third-ranked channel | **retrievability already demonstrated on one side**: the acquirer is one of the two carriers whose filings a third party pulled and compared across a procedure change in an open state |

**The third row earns its place on a different axis from the first.** Its channel
is weaker, since a domestic acquirer moves scale and cost of funds without
bringing a visibly foreign procedure. **But it is the only row where one party's
filings have already been retrieved by someone else and reported.** For a first
attempt that is worth more than channel rank, because it removes the one failure
mode that would waste the whole attempt.

## What is now the binding step

**Read two filings.** The last personal auto rating plan the acquired business
filed in one open state before April 2021, and the first the acquirer filed for
that book after it. **Count the distinct class values in each.** The pre-check and
the criterion are the same two numbers, so one retrieval answers both.

## Limits

1. **No filing has been pulled and no count exists.** Every number above is a date
   or a description.
2. **The second row is named from recall and its dates are not verified.** It is
   in the table so the census is not a census of one, and it is marked.
3. **Which open state to use has not been chosen.** The one with a demonstrated
   window is the obvious first try, and whether both parties wrote personal auto
   there is unchecked.

# B30-23, chunk four: inside the filing system, and what the object actually looks like

**Read 2026-09-03.** The public filing search was opened and queried for the
first time. **Chunks one and two argued the object was retrievable from a
published method note; this block confirms it directly and returns four things
the census did not have.**

## 1  The type code the criterion runs on

**`19.0001 Private Passenger Auto (PPA)`**. It appears as its own column value in
the results, so the filings the criterion wants can be separated from pet,
recreational vehicle, legal services and interline filings without opening any of
them.

## 2  The acquired side's underwriting entities, which the census was missing

A search on the acquired group's name in one open state returns **73 property and
casualty filings**, and the filing titles name the affiliated set explicitly:
**the property and casualty company, the general company, the direct company and
the group company**, filed together on shared rule filings. **The lead entity
carries NAIC company code 39950.** The census had a corporate transaction and no
filing entities; it now has both.

## 3  What a result row carries, and it is enough to navigate on

Company name, NAIC company code, insurance product name, **sub type of
insurance**, **filing type** (form, rule, rate, or a combination), filing status,
and the tracking number. **The criterion needs a filing whose type includes rate
or rule**, because a form filing carries policy language and not a rating plan.
Both kinds are present and distinguishable at the list level.

## 4  An operational constraint, and it shapes how wide this arm can go

**The search interface does not accept programmatic form filling.** Entering a
date resets the form, and the rows-per-page control does not respond to a
synthetic click. **This is consistent with the use agreement, which prohibits
bypassing the intended workflow in order to automate downloading.**

> **So retrieval here is a hand operation, a few filings at a time.**

**That is a limit on breadth and not on this criterion.** The criterion needs
**two filings**, the last one before a transfer and the first one after. A hand
operation delivers two. **What it does not deliver is a census across many
transfers**, so if this arm ever wants that, it needs a different access route
and the design should say so before anyone starts clicking.

## What is still not done

**No filing has been opened and no count exists.** The next step is unchanged and
is now a short one: open one rate or rule filing on `19.0001` from before April
2021, find the rating plan attachment, and count the distinct class values.

## Limits

1. **One state, one side.** The acquiring group's filings were not searched.
2. **Dates were not obtained.** The result list carries no submission date column,
   so ordering the filings in time needs either the refine-search date fields,
   which reset, or opening filings one at a time.
3. **Everything above is navigation.** No rating plan has been read.

# B30-23, chunk five: two things that would have corrupted the reading, and the state has to be chosen again

**Read 2026-09-03.** Two findings from opening filings by hand. **Both would have
produced a wrong count silently rather than an error.**

## 1  The displayed company name is the current one, not the one on the filing

A filing submitted in August 2013 and withdrawn that October lists three
companies, and **two of them carry the acquirer's name at the acquired group's
own headquarters address**. There were no such entities at that address in 2013.
**The interface shows today's legal names against historical filings.** The
acquired entities were renamed after the 2021 transfer and the new names were
applied backwards through the display.

> **So the acquired party's pre-transfer filings appear under the acquiring
> party's name.**

**That lands exactly on this criterion's path**, which is a before-and-after
comparison of two parties. Keying on the displayed name mixes the two sides while
every other field stays correct: the tracking number, the dates and the
attachments all belong to the filing, and only the name belongs to today.
**Identifiers disagreeing with the objects they index while all counts stay right
is the twelfth category error, and this is a clean instance of it.**

**The key is the NAIC company code plus the filing date.** Codes are stable and
names are not, and the search form accepts the code. **Three are now known: the
lead property and casualty entity, the direct entity and the general entity of
the acquired group.**

## 2  The state that demonstrates retrievability is the state its own reform confounds

The acquired group's personal auto filings in the chosen state **stop around
2016**, on a hand inspection of the visible list. Two readings follow and only the
second is load-bearing.

**The weaker one**: the group may simply have stopped writing personal auto in
that state, which was the most expensive auto system in the country before its
2019 reform and one that national carriers avoided. **Checkable by code search,
not checked.**

**The load-bearing one, and it holds either way**: the last pre-transfer filing
predates the state's own 2019 reform. **That reform is itself a procedure change.**
So a before-and-after comparison across April 2021 in that state spans both the
reform and the acquisition, **and the criterion cannot separate them.**

> **This is not a thin sample. The carrier is confounded.**

**And the irony is exact.** That state was chosen because a published
investigation had compared filings across its 2019 reform, which is what
demonstrated the retention window. **The thing that demonstrated retrievability is
the thing that spoils the reading.** The selection reason and the disqualifying
reason are the same fact.

## 3  A third condition on state selection, added here

| condition | source |
|---|---|
| the filings and their attachments are public | chunk one |
| the window reaches before the transfer | chunk two |
| **both parties wrote the line in that state** | the criterion needs three filings, two of them from one party |
| **the state ran no auto reform of its own between 2019 and 2022** | **this block** |

**The fourth is free and comes first**, because it disqualifies on public
knowledge before any search is run.

## Limits

1. **The renaming is inferred from an address and a date**, not from a corporate
   filing. It is firm enough to change the search key and it has not been
   confirmed against a name-change record.
2. **The stop around 2016 is from a hand inspection of a paged list**, not a
   complete enumeration by code.
3. **Still no rating plan read and no count.**

# B30-23, chunk six: the state was changed, both sides of the transfer are on the disk, and the migration is a named document

**Read 2026-09-03.** Chunk five disqualified the first state because its own 2019
reform sits inside the comparison window. **The arm was moved to a second state
that ran no auto reform between 2019 and 2022, and on that state both sides of
the transfer are present.** Filings were opened by hand; **no attachment has been
downloaded and no count exists.**

## 1  The key that says which side a filing came from, and it is a field

Chunk five keyed on the NAIC company code because names are current rather than
historical. **The address is current too**, which chunk five did not know: an
entity that belonged to the acquiring group long before the transfer now displays
the acquired group's headquarters, because operations were consolidated there.
**So neither the name nor the address distinguishes the two sides.**

**The SERFF tracking number prefix does, and it is stable.**

| prefix | filer |
|---|---|
| **`METX-`** | the acquired group's filing organisation |
| **`FAIG-`** | the acquiring group's |

**That turns a question of inference into reading one field.** The pre-transfer
filing found here carries `METX-`; the post-transfer ones carry `FAIG-`. **Use the
prefix for the side and the code for the entity.**

## 2  The pre-transfer object

| | |
|---|---|
| tracking | `METX-132665343` |
| submitted / disposed | 8 January 2021 / **29 January 2021** |
| type | **Rate/Rule**, `19.0001 Private Passenger Auto (PPA)` |
| entity | NAIC **26298** |
| status | closed with no further information required, which is acceptance in this state |
| **the object** | **`TX Auto Met360 Rate Pages`, rate action Replacement** |

**That is the last filed rating plan of the acquired book before the transfer**,
which is one of the criterion's three quantities.

## 3  The post-transfer filings, and what they contain

Two filings dated 17 November 2021, both **rejected** on 1 December. **Rejected
means neither took effect**, so the operative post-transfer manual is in a later
filing that has not been found yet. **What they contain is nonetheless the whole
shape of the transfer.**

**The migration is a named document.** Both carry a filing memorandum whose name
begins with a phrase naming the two parties and the direction, followed by a
version date one month after closing. **The party wrote down that it was moving
one book onto the other's procedure, and dated it.**

**Each filing carries the acquirer's own manual and its replacement side by
side**, the current versions from 2019 and the replacements from December 2021,
plus marked-up copies. **So two of the criterion's three quantities sit in one
retrieval**: the acquirer's own count, and the count the acquired book was moved
to.

**And one of the two narrows the object to a single named rule.** Its memorandum
and its mark-ups are all about one numbered rule in the manual, with a separate
mark-up for each of three manuals at different page ranges. **A named rule, a
published difference, a date, inside a migration the party labelled itself.**

## 4  What to search next, stated as a query rather than a plan

**Prefix `FAIG-`, this state, sub type `19.0001`, submitted after December 2021,
disposition anything other than rejected.** That is the operative post-transfer
manual and it completes the third quantity.

## Limits

1. **Nothing has been downloaded and nothing counted.** Everything above is a
   filing summary page.
2. **The rejected pair may have been rejected on form rather than on substance**,
   and no correspondence is attached to either to say which. The refiling has not
   been located.
3. **The pre-transfer filing is a rate-page replacement, the post-transfer ones
   are rule manuals.** Whether the same table exists in both, which the criterion
   requires, is unchecked and is the first thing to check on opening them.

# B30-23, chunk seven: all three quantities are named documents, and the partition inventory came free with the attachment names

**Read 2026-09-03.** Chunk six left the post-transfer side incomplete because the
two filings found there were rejected. **The accepted one has been located.**
Filing summaries only; **nothing downloaded and nothing counted.**

## The pair, and both sides were accepted

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| tracking | `METX-132665343` | **`FAIG-133029101`** |
| submitted | 8 January 2021 | 28 October 2021 |
| **disposed** | **29 January 2021** | **9 March 2022** |
| status | closed, no further information required | **the same, which is acceptance here** |
| entity | NAIC **26298** | **NAIC 26298, the same** |
| line | `19.0001`, Rate/Rule | `19.0001`, Rate/Rule |
| filer | the acquired group's organisation | **the acquiring group's** |

**Same entity, same state, same line, both accepted, and the change of control
falls between the two disposition dates.** The prefix carries the side, which is
what chunk six established, and here it does the work: the entity code does not
change across the transfer and the prefix does.

**The product name says the migration out loud too.** The before filing prices a
product whose name carries the acquired brand; the after filing's own exhibits
name a product carrying the acquirer's. **The same book, renamed.**

## The partition inventory, obtained without opening anything

The accepted filing lists its rate and rule attachments by name, **and the names
carry both the partition and its extent**:

| table | pages |
|---|---|
| semi-annual base rate | 1 |
| personal credit factor | 40 |
| **classification factors** | **450** |
| household composition | 20 |
| a usage-based rule page | 1 |
| prior insurance tier factors | 19 |

**That is the class-line inventory of the post-transfer procedure, free, off the
attachment names.** Five named partitions plus a base. **The criterion counts
distinct values inside one of these**, and the classification table at 450 rate
pages is where the count will be largest and where the two sides are most likely
to differ.

## The third quantity is in the same filing as the second

Two marked-up documents are attached: **one marks up the acquired book's rate
pages, and one marks up the acquiring group's own auto manual**, both dated the
day after submission. **So the acquirer's own count and the count the book was
moved to are in a single retrieval**, which is what chunk three predicted and
what makes this pair cheap.

## The condition that is still unverified and would void the pair

**The before side is one document; the after side is six separately named
tables.** The criterion compares **the same table** on both sides. **Whether the
before document contains these same partitions is unchecked**, and it is the
first thing to read on opening. **If it does not, this pair does not support the
criterion and a different table or a different case is needed.** The branch is
reachable and is not being treated as unlikely.


## Superseded 2026-09-03 by chunk nine, in this scope

**What does not survive.** This block reads the post-transfer filing as the
acquired book moved onto the acquiring party's procedure, and reads two of the
marked-up attachments as the acquirer's own manual and the difference against it.
**The filing's own introduction says otherwise**: it is a rate revision to a
program the acquired party had approved in 2018, and it names the January 2021
filing as its own immediate predecessor. **The two filings are consecutive
revisions of one program, not two sides of a procedure replacement.** The manual
attached under the acquirer's brand belongs to the renamed acquired entity and not
to the acquiring group.

**What does survive.** The pair is real, both filings are accepted, the entity and
the state and the line match, and the partition inventory taken off the attachment
names is correct. **What the pair is not is a before and after of this arm's
treatment.**

## Limits

1. **Nothing downloaded, nothing counted.** Every statement above comes from
   filing summary pages and attachment file names.
2. **Page counts are not value counts.** Four hundred and fifty rate pages is an
   extent, not the number of distinct values on them, and the difference between
   those two is the whole point of the criterion.
3. **The acquired product's own table structure is inferred from one file name**
   and could be a bundle of the same partitions under one cover.
