  SITE. The RBI notifications themselves were NOT retrieved, and the indelible-ink
  measure, which was the predicted form of the once-only device, does not appear here at
  all.**

- **Revista de Economia Politica (SciELO)**, Plano Collor. Quoting the March 1990
  measures: savings "poderao ser sacados uma unica vez ate o limite de Cz$ 50 mil";
  short-term and overnight applications limited to "Cr$ 25 mil ou 20% do total, valendo o
  maior valor"; "Para efeito do calculo dos limites de conversao ..., considerar-se-a o
  total das conversoes efetuadas em nome de um unico titular em uma mesma instituicao
  financeira"; blocked balances converted from 16 September 1991 in twelve equal monthly
  instalments; and prices and wages converted at par, one cruzado novo per cruzeiro.
  **Peer-reviewed journal quoting the measures. Medida Provisoria 168 and Lei 8.024
  themselves were NOT retrieved. The per-holder aggregation clause is the load-bearing
  quotation of the chunk.**

- **The Irrawaddy**, The day three Myanmar banknotes suddenly became worthless. The BSPP
  demonetising the 25, 35 and 75 kyat notes on 5 September 1987, those accounting for
  around 80 per cent of money in circulation, and the government having "offered no
  compensation at all" in contrast with 1964 and 1985. **Independent Myanmar outlet,
  retrospective. No exchange procedure existed to document.**

- **P2Tel**, Redenominasi dan sanering jaman Bung Karno. The 25 August 1959 sanering
  cutting the Rp500 and Rp1000 notes to 10 per cent of their value, and the 13 December
  1965 cut of three zeros. **Trade/association blog. It carries NO deposit-freeze terms,
  so the Indonesian cells for the per-person limit and the treatment of the excess are
  recorded UNSCORED.**

- **Michael J. Meurer and Ben Depoorter**, Price Discrimination and Intellectual
  Property, 2019, Boston University School of Law faculty scholarship. "Successful price
  discrimination relies on sellers sorting buyers in terms of willingness to pay; those
  willing to pay more are charged more"; "a price discriminator must block arbitrage that
  could erode the price differential between favored and disfavored buyers"; and Pigou's
  three degrees. **Law review scholarship. Only these two sentences and the three-degree
  classification are read; Pigou's own text is NOT retrieved and the classification is
  taken at second hand.**

- **Dirk Bergemann, Benjamin Brooks and Stephen Morris**, The Limits of Price
  Discrimination, American Economic Review 105(3), 2015, 921-957. Abstract read verbatim,
  plus the result that at most 2K segments suffice to reach any point of the welfare
  triangle where K is the number of distinct valuations. **ABSTRACT AND ONE RESULT ONLY;
  the body is NOT read. Nothing in this chunk rests on the body.**

- **Timothy Besley and Torsten Persson**, Taxation and Development, 2013. "To collect
  trade taxes just requires being able to observe trade flows at major shipping ports.
  Although trade taxes may encourage smuggling, this is a much easier proposition than
  collecting income taxes, which requires major investments in enforcement and compliance
  structures throughout the entire economy"; "large-scale compliance with the income tax
  required states to build a tax administration and implement withholding at source"; and
  the cross-sectional fact that rich countries rely more on income taxes and poor
  countries more on trade taxes. **Working paper by named economists. This is the source
  that pre-empts prediction two, which is therefore withdrawn unrun.**

- **James C. Scott**, Seeing Like a State: How Certain Schemes to Improve the Human
  Condition Have Failed, Yale University Press, 1998. The legibility argument: cadastral
  surveys, permanent surnames and standard measures making society administrable.
  **PARAPHRASE ONLY. Scott's own wording was NOT obtained, so no sentence of his is
  quoted anywhere in this line; the argument is recorded as existing, nothing rests on
  its exact form.**

- **House of Commons Library**, Student loans: Interest rates and repayment thresholds
  FAQs, research briefing CBP-10654. Plan 1 threshold GBP 26,900 and 9 per cent, interest
  the lower of RPI or bank base plus 1 per cent; Plan 2 threshold GBP 29,385 and 9 per
  cent, written off after 30 years, interest RPI plus 3 per cent during study and
  post-study "RPI if income is GBP 29,385 or less, rising on a sliding scale to RPI plus
  3% when income is GBP 52,885 or more"; Plan 3 postgraduate threshold GBP 21,000 and 6
  per cent at RPI plus 3 per cent; Plan 4 threshold GBP 33,795 and 9 per cent; Plan 5
  threshold GBP 25,000 and 9 per cent, written off after 40 years, interest RPI only; and
  "Loan repayments taken out of an employee's salary at the same time as tax and National
  Insurance". **OFFICIAL: the UK Parliament's own research service. Write-off periods for
  Plans 1, 3 and 4 are NOT stated in this briefing and were not obtained elsewhere.**

- **HM Revenue and Customs**, Rates and allowances for Air Passenger Duty, gov.uk. The
  four destination bands (Domestic; Band A 0 to 2,000 miles; Band B 2,001 to 5,500; Band
  C over 5,500, measured London to the destination capital), and the three rate
  definitions verbatim: reduced "For travel in the lowest class of travel available on
  the plane for seat pitches less than 1.016 metres (40 inches)", standard "For travel in
  any other class of travel or where the seat pitch is more than 1.016 metres (40
  inches)", higher "For travel in planes of 20 tonnes or more equipped to carry fewer
  than 19 passengers"; plus the exemption for direct long-haul flights from Northern
  Ireland airports. **OFFICIAL, the tax authority's own guidance. This is the source of
  the load-bearing point that the state's class is defined on seat pitch, not on the
  carrier's cabin name.**

- **FCC Aviation**, UK APD: The Ultimate Guide. Collection by the operator under the
  Finance Act 1994 with monthly or annual HMRC declaration by the 22nd day after the
  accounting period; the all-business and single-class edge cases; the condition that
  preferential treatment applies where the passenger "pay[s] extra for the better seat"
  or where seats are not "available on a 'first-come first-served' basis"; chargeable
  passengers on fixed-wing aircraft above 5.7 tonnes MTOW; and the exemptions for infants
  without allocated seats, children under 16 in the lowest class, and connecting
  passengers whose first leg departed outside the UK within 24 hours on a single ticket.
  **AVIATION TAX SERVICE PROVIDER's guide, not an HMRC notice. The edge cases and the
  allocation test rest on it alone; the bands, rates and NI exemption come from GOV.UK.**

- **Joel Slemrod and Wojciech Kopczuk**, The Optimal Elasticity of Taxable Income,
  Journal of Public Economics, 2002. Abstract verbatim: "The strength of the behavioral
  response to a tax rate change depends on the environment individuals operate in, and
  may be manipulated by instruments controlled by the government. We first derive a
  measure of the social benefit to affecting this elasticity. The paper then examines
  this effect in the solution to the optimal income taxation problem when such an
  instrument is available, first in a general model and then in an example when the
  government chooses the income tax base." **ABSTRACT AND ONE PARAPHRASED SENTENCE ONLY;
  the body is NOT read. This is the source that pre-empts the second half of the
  eighth-device claim, and it is more general than that claim.**

- **Henrik Jacobsen Kleven**, Bunching, Annual Review of Economics, and **Kleven and
  Waseem**, Behavioral Responses to Notches: Evidence from Pakistani Tax Records; with
  **Saez 2010** as the origin of the method. **TITLES, VENUE AND SUBJECT ONLY; none of
  these is read. They are cited to establish that the physical-threshold response is an
  established field, which is sufficient for withdrawing a novelty claim and
  insufficient for anything else.**

- **George A. Akerlof**, The Economics of "Tagging" as Applied to the Optimal Income Tax,
  Welfare Programs, and Manpower Planning, American Economic Review 68, 1978. **TITLE AND
  SUBJECT ONLY, NOT READ.**

- **UK Soft Drinks Industry Levy evaluations**, including an interrupted time series
  analysis of the soft drink marketplace 2017-2020 and Institute for Fiscal Studies
  analysis. **TITLES ONLY, NOT READ; cited to establish that the reformulation response
  to a physical threshold has been measured independently.**

- **Jean-Charles Rochet and Lars Stole**, The Economics of Multidimensional Screening,
  chapter 5, World Congress 2000. "Both settings give rise to identical surplus functions
  and partial derivatives with respect to type, and hence have identical optimal price
  schedules", with Mussa and Rosen (1978) and Mirrlees (1971, 1976) positioned as
  applications of one screening theory. **ONE SENTENCE AND THE POSITIONING ONLY; the
  chapter is NOT read. It is cited to establish that the monopolist's nonlinear pricing
  problem and the optimal income tax problem are treated as one problem, which is
  sufficient to withdraw a novelty claim and insufficient for anything else.**

- **Ramsey problem**, encyclopedia entry. "Frank P. Ramsey discovered this principle in
  1927 in the context of optimal taxation: the more elastic the demand or supply, the
  smaller the optimal tax", and "The rule was later applied by Marcel Boiteux (1956) to
  natural monopolies". **TERTIARY. Ramsey 1927 and Boiteux 1956 are NOT read. No verbatim
  statement of formal equivalence was obtained; what is established here is the
  historical sequence and the joint name Ramsey-Boiteux pricing.**

- **Eugenio J. Miravete**, Price Discrimination (Theory), The New Palgrave Dictionary of
  Economics, 2008. Consulted; its bibliography cites Mirrlees 1971 and 1976 inside a
  price-discrimination entry. **NO EQUIVALENCE STATEMENT WAS VISIBLE in the accessible
  portion; recorded as suggestive of the link and load-bearing for nothing.**

- **Richard A. Posner**, Taxation by Regulation, 1971. **NOT RETRIEVED. Flagged as a
  probable third occupation of the same terrain, to be obtained before any claim about
  what the terrain does or does not contain.**

- **AQA A-Level Economics notes (TutorChase)**, Conditions for Price Discrimination. The
  three stated necessary conditions verbatim: "The firm must have some degree of monopoly
  power, meaning it is a price maker rather than a price taker"; "The firm must be able to
  separate consumers into distinct groups based on their price elasticity of demand"; "The
  firm must prevent or limit resale between groups". **TEACHING MATERIAL, cited as a
  statement of the textbook conditions, not as a research source. It is used only to fix
  the standard three-condition formulation against which this line's corpus is compared;
  a primary statement (Pigou 1920) is NOT obtained.**

- **Nick O'Donovan**, One-off Wealth Taxes: Theory and Evidence, Wealth Tax Commission
  Evidence Paper no. 7, 2020, wealthandpolicy.com. The unanticipated one-off levy being
  non-distortionary because "the fact that the tax is unheralded means that it will not
  alter economic behaviour in the run-up to its announcement"; the failure of the
  mechanism once anticipated; the three design elements of surprise, backdating (with
  "a retrospective approach would face serious public law challenges") and
  anti-forestalling provisions; and the currency-reform valuation anchors, France's
  Impot de Solidarite Nationale using "4 June 1945 (the date of the French post-war
  currency reform)" and West Germany's Lastenausgleichsgesetz using "the June 1948
  currency reform which introduced the Deutsche Mark". **EVIDENCE PAPER for a named
  commission. EXTRACTED SUMMARY ONLY; the body is NOT read, and whether it treats the
  1948 conversion's own graded terms as a levy in their own right is therefore UNKNOWN.
  That question decides how much of chunks seventeen, eighteen and twenty survives.**

- **Gesetz Nr. 61, Erstes Gesetz zur Neuordnung des Geldwesens (Waehrungsgesetz),
  20 June 1948**, read in the original German at verfassungen.de. Paragraph 6 on the
  per-head amount ("Jeder Einwohner des Waehrungsgebiets erhaelt im Umtausch gegen
  Altgeldnoten desselben Nennbetrages bis zu sechzig Deutsche Mark in bar", paid as no
  more than forty immediately and the rest within two months); paragraph 17(1) on the
  Geschaeftsbetrag ("sechzig Deutsche Mark je Arbeitnehmer, hoechstens jedoch eine
  Deutsche Mark fuer jede Reichsmark Altgeldguthaben"); paragraphs 15 and 16 on the
  one-sixth and one-twelfth of actual revenues for the Laender and for the railway and
  postal administrations; and paragraph 1(2) on certain military and Rentenbank notes at
  one tenth of face. **PRIMARY, a legal-text archive. This is the source of every
  paragraph quoted in the chunk.**

- **Gabler Banklexikon**, Waehrungsreform. "Die Umstellung erfolgte grundsaetzlich im
  Verhaeltnis 10:1"; "Altguthaben wurden im Verhaeltnis 100:6,5 umgestellt"; "Loehne und
  Gehaelter, Mieten, Pachten, Renten, Pensionen, Beitraege und Leistungen der
  Sozialversicherung 1:1"; and the 60 DM Kopfgeld in two instalments of 40 and 20.
  **REFERENCE LEXICON, not the statute. The Umstellungsgesetz itself was NOT obtained:
  gesetze-im-internet.de failed on robots and buzer.de returned 403. The three ratios
  that give the corpus its largest spread therefore rest on this secondary source and
  must be replaced with the statute before they are cited anywhere.**

- **Umstellungsgesetz (Drittes Gesetz zur Neuordnung des Geldwesens), 27 June 1948**,
  paragraphs 1, 2 and 16 read in the original German. Paragraph 1 defining Gruppe I to
  IV, including Gruppe III's list running from territorial treasuries and the railway and
  postal administrations through "der NSDAP, ihrer Gliederungen und angeschlossenen
  Verbaende und aller sonstigen von der Militaerregierung aufgeloesten Organisationen",
  the Reich, the Reichsbank and the war-financing companies; Gruppe IV covering holders
  domiciled outside the currency area unless taxable there and, "ohne Ruecksicht auf die
  Person des Kontoinhabers", accounts accumulating denazification Suehnebetraege; and the
  definition of Familie by an eighteenth birthday on 21 June 1948. Paragraph 2 on the
  ten-to-one conversion of Gruppe I with half free and half on a blocked Festkonto to be
  decided within ninety days, and on Gruppe II balances extinguishing on 10 July 1948.
  Paragraph 16(1) on the general ten-to-one debt conversion and 16(3) referring debtor
  gains to the Lastenausgleich. **PRIMARY STATUTE TEXT, supplied by hand: the two online
  copies were blocked, gesetze-im-internet.de on robots and buzer.de with a 403.
  Paragraph 9, which governs Gruppe III, is NOT among the paragraphs obtained.**

- **Deutsche Bundesbank**, Waehrungsreform 1948, bundesbank.de, supplied by hand. Current
  payments including wages, salaries, taxes, rents and social-insurance and other
  pensions converted at 1:1; the Umstellungsgesetz of 27 June 1948 converting private
  bank balances at 10:1 with the 40 DM Kopfbetraege credited against them and only half
  freely disposable; the Festkontengesetz of October 1948 setting 100:6.5; and "Die
  Militaerregierung verordnete schliesslich die ersatzlose Streichung der restlichen
  Guthaben". **OFFICIAL, the central bank's own account. The Festkontengesetz itself and
  the cancellation order are NOT obtained.**

- **Gesetz Nr. 65 / Verordnung Nr. 175, Viertes Gesetz zur Neuordnung des Geldwesens
  (Ergaenzung des Umstellungsgesetzes)**, American zone 4 October 1948, British zone 30

  seven of every ten Deutsche Mark credited to a Festkonto struck "mit Wirkung vom Tage
  der Gutschrift"; two of every ten transferred to the corresponding Freikonto; the
  remainder available only for investment in medium- or long-term securities under
  regulations to be issued by the Allied Banking Commission before 1 January 1949.
  **PRIMARY STATUTE TEXT. With Umstellungsgesetz paragraph 2(1) it yields the 100 to 6.5
  ratio by arithmetic, so the Gabler and Bundesbank restatements of that ratio are no
  longer relied on. The four implementing ordinances are NOT obtained.**

- **Gesetz ueber den Lastenausgleich (Lastenausgleichsgesetz, LAG), 14 August 1952,

  to 197); Dritter Teil Ausgleichsleistungen including "Waehrungsausgleich fuer
  Sparguthaben Vertriebener" at paragraph 304. **TABLE OF CONTENTS ONLY. No section text
  is read, so no rate, term or amount from this statute is asserted anywhere in this
  line.**

- **A summary of the LAG generated by another language model** was supplied in the same
  batch and is recorded here as NOT USED. Another model's output is not a source: it may

- **Umstellungsgesetz paragraph 9**, supplied by hand: "Soweit dieses Gesetz und die dazu
  ergehenden Durchfuehrungsverordnungen nicht etwas anderes bestimmen, begruenden die

  Neugeldguthaben. Diese Altgeldguthaben erloeschen." **PRIMARY STATUTE TEXT. It closes

- **31 CFR Part 1021 Subpart C**, ecfr.gov. The casino currency transaction report for

- **Parallel session of this project**, relaying: the Indian 2016 two-channel comparison

- **Lastenausgleichsgesetz, consolidated text**, gesetze-im-internet.de/lag, read through
  the browser. Paragraph 1 stating the law's purpose as compensating expulsion and war
  damage "sowie die Milderung von Haerten, die infolge der Neuordnung des Geldwesens ...
  eingetreten sind"; paragraph 3 naming the three levies, the one-off Vermoegensabgabe

  text. It is the AMENDED version, last amended in 2026, not the 1952 original; the levy

- **Perry Mehrling**, The Inherent Hierarchy of Money, 25 January 2012, seminar paper,
  sites.bu.edu/perry. The four-layer hierarchy Gold, Currency, Deposits, Securities; the
  qualitative differences stated as promises ("National currencies ... are promises to
  pay gold. Bank deposits are promises to pay currency ... securities are promises to pay
  currency (or deposits)"); the hierarchy emerging "organically from the logic of its
  normal functioning" and being "not something simply imposed from the outside"; and
  position in the hierarchy being set by the market makers at each level, banks and
  central banks "quoting prices at which they are willing to convert credit into money
  and vice versa". **SEMINAR PAPER, extract only; Mehrling's books and articles are NOT
  read. It is cited to establish that the qualitative-difference-between-claims idea is
  occupied and that its author explicitly scopes it to hierarchies that are not imposed
  from outside, which is what separates it from this line's corpus.**

- **Decreto 1570/2001 (Argentina)**, 1 December 2001, ENTIDADES FINANCIERAS,
  argentina.gob.ar/normativa/nacional/norma-70355/texto. Article 2 verbatim:
  *"Los retiros en efectivo que superen los PESOS DOSCIENTOS CINCUENTA ($ 250) o
  DOLARES ESTADOUNIDENSES DOSCIENTOS CINCUENTA (U$S 250) por semana, por parte del
  titular, o de los titulares que actúen en forma conjunta o indistinta, del total de
  sus cuentas en cada entidad financiera"*; Article 5, *"Las entidades no podrán
  obstaculizar la transferencia o disposición de los fondos entre cuentas … ni percibir
  comisión alguna por la transferencia electrónica"*; Article 7, the export of foreign
  banknotes and coined precious metals is prohibited except through supervised entities
  or below U$S 1,000. **OFFICIAL. Read for the per-holder aggregation wording, for the
  absence of any provision touching domestic banknotes held outside banks, and for the
  fact that movement inside the deposit system is expressly left unrestricted.**

- **Decreto 214/2002 (Argentina)**, 4 February 2002, REORDENAMIENTO DEL SISTEMA
  FINANCIERO, argentina.gob.ar/normativa/nacional/decreto-214-2002-72017/texto.
  Article 2, deposits in the financial system convert *"a razón de PESOS UNO CON
  CUARENTA CENTAVOS ($ 1,40) por cada DÓLAR ESTADOUNIDENSE"*; Article 3, debts owed to

  Article 8, obligations unconnected to the financial system convert at *"UN DOLAR
  ESTADOUNIDENSE (U$S 1) = UN PESO ($ 1)"* with either party able to ask for an
  equitable price adjustment; Article 4, the CER index applies. **OFFICIAL. Three
  classes written on one currency unit; two share a scalar and differ in an attached
  term, which is why this case is the counting law's first narrow pass.**

- **Some European Currency and Exchange Reforms**, Princeton PRI, pri.princeton.edu/pdf/E7.pdf.
  For France, p. 6: *"the conversion was not coupled with a blocking program"*, with
  footnote 10 giving the note circulation falling 104 billion francs between May and
  August 1945 and returning to within 3 billion francs of the May level by November,
  attributed in part to *"failure of persons to turn in hoards"*. For Belgium, p. 5:
  *"All but 33 percent of the currency in circulation … were blocked"*, of the blocked
  amounts 40 percent released under licence, the rest frozen indefinitely; and
  *"All but 10 percent of the bank deposits were blocked"*. Also covers Austria,
  Czechoslovakia, Denmark, Greece, Italy, the Netherlands, Norway, Yugoslavia and

  for the finding that France 1945 wrote no differential at all, which is what makes

- **Ordonnance n° 45-1820 du 15 août 1945** instituant un impôt de solidarité nationale
  et édictant diverses mesures de simplifications fiscales, Legifrance JORFTEXT000000701436.
  **TITLE AND CITATION ONLY. The article text was NOT read: Legifrance returns 403 to

- **Impôt de solidarité nationale**, fr.wikipedia.org and its Wikimonde mirror. The two

- **La démonétisation des billets en juin 1945**, issoire-philatelie.com. The ordinance of
  4 June 1945, demonetisation effective 15 June 1945, a twelve-day window, and the list of

- **PERPU No. 2 Tahun 1959** and **PERPU No. 3 Tahun 1959 tentang Pembekuan Sebagian Dari
  Simpanan Pada Bank-Bank**, peraturan.bpk.go.id, Details/53804 and Home/Details/53808.
  PERPU 3 dated 24 August 1959, in force 25 August 1959. **OFFICIAL DATABASE, METADATA
  ONLY. Both instruments are held there as scanned PDFs (Download/43209/perpu 003 1959.pdf
  for PERPU 3), which return binary to a fetch. The articles were NOT read. The deposit

- **Bank Indonesia, Report for the Year 1960-1965**, seadelt.net Document_ID-156_No-01.pdf.
  *"decreasing the value of bank notes of Rp 1000 and Rp 500 denominations to respectively
  Rp 100 and Rp 50"*; and *"the blocking of part of bank savings. The amount of blocked
  balance deposited as Government profit at the time totalled Rp 8.264 million"*, later

  central bank's own report. It establishes the note cut to ten percent of face and the
  existence and aggregate size of the deposit freeze, and it does not give the threshold.**

- **Operation Gutt: A gamble that paid off**, National Bank of Belgium museum,
  museum.nbb.be. The exchange ran Monday 9 to Friday 13 October 1944, announced by radio
  on 6 October; all denominations from BEF 100 up were withdrawn while BEF 50 and below
  stayed in circulation; *"BEF 2 000 per member of a household (collected by the head of
  the household)"*; the surplus went to a special frozen account; and account holders
  received either *"10% of the amount held in their accounts on 8 October 1944"* or
  *"the entire balance of the account on 9 May 1940"*. **CENTRAL BANK MUSEUM, not the
  arrete-loi itself. Belgium 1944 was read without being registered, so it enters this
  line as corpus only and scores nothing.**

- **Kenneth Burdett and Kenneth L. Judd**, Equilibrium Price Dispersion, *Econometrica* 51(4),
  July 1983. Consumers identical, firms identical, homogeneous good, constant marginal cost `r`;
  *"What appears to be crucial for equilibrium price dispersion is an ex post heterogeneity in
  consumer information"*; with `0 < q1 < 1` the equilibrium `F` is *"continuous with connected
  support"*, so no interior equilibrium has finitely many prices; and the paper's own novelty
  claim, *"a dispersed price equilibrium can exist with nonsequential search, even when all
  consumers face the same cost of search. To the authors' knowledge this result is new"*, with
  neither *"costs, tastes, nor rationality"* heterogeneity needed. **ABSTRACT AND KEY SENTENCES
  ONLY, full text not read. Cited to establish that dispersion without valuation heterogeneity
  is already occupied by a theorem, that its object is cross-seller rather than cross-buyer, that
  its support is a continuum rather than a finite integer, and that its own parameter reading on
  a published-schedule carrier is the single-price case.**

- **National Minimum Wage statistics**, House of Commons Library research briefing CBP-7735,
  researchbriefings.files.parliament.uk. The full statutory age-band history: two bands in 1999
  (22+, 18-21); the 16-17 rate created October 2004; the apprentice rate created and the adult
  threshold moved 22 to 21 in October 2010; the National Living Wage created at 25+ in April
  2016; its threshold moved to 23+ in April 2021 and to 21+ in April 2024, merging away the
  21-22 band; and the September 2024 remit to the Low Pay Commission to *"gradually abolish the
  18-20 rate ... so that the NLW eventually applies to those aged 18+"*. Also that ASHE-based
  coverage and underpayment are reported by band. **OFFICIAL PARLIAMENTARY BRIEFING. Read for
  the written partition and its amendment dates. Two figures from it are named in the chunk as
  already seen and barred from carrying any criterion.**

- **Henrik Kleven**, Bunching, *Annual Review of Economics* 8, 2016, eml.berkeley.edu preprint.
  The definition, *"kink points, discrete changes in the slope of choice sets, and notch points,
  discrete changes in the level of choice sets"*, and the statement that applications exploit
  *"not only discontinuities created by government policies, but also discontinuities in private
  sector incentive schemes"*. The catalogue of settings is listed in the chunk. **SURVEY, read
  for its enumeration of carriers where a published schedule writes a class boundary. Its
  econometrics is not used. The load-bearing observation is that the method presupposes the
  written partition is the operative one, which is the proposition this line tests.**

- **World Tax Indicators**, International Center for Public Policy, Georgia State University,
  icepp.gsu.edu. PIT dataset downloadable in Excel and Stata with documentation, free on
  registration; time-varying measures of structural progressivity and tax complexity.
  **CONTENTS NOT VERIFIED. The page does not say whether every bracket threshold and rate is
  included or only summary indicators, so under `D26` no cell is filled from it and no claim is
  made about its usability until the documentation is in hand.**

- **Low Pay Commission**, uprating report for 2026, assets.publishing.service.gov.uk. Coverage in
  April 2025, the share of jobs paid at or below the applicable rate: NLW aged 21 and over `6.1`
  per cent on 1,756 thousand jobs; 18-20 `15.1` per cent on 162 thousand; 16-17 `21.8` per cent
  on 68 thousand; apprentices `17.2` per cent on 32 thousand. Rates from April 2026: NLW `12.71`,
  18-20 rate `10.85`, 16-17 rate `8.00`, apprentice `8.00`. **OFFICIAL. Every one of these
  coverage figures is computed against the band's own rate, so each is internal to the written
  partition and none of them can test whether the observed partition equals it. Recorded in the
  chunk as a circular quantity that was nearly used as positive evidence.**

- **ONS**, ASHE Table 6, Earnings and hours worked by age group, ons.gov.uk. Free ZIP downloads,
  1997 to 2025. **The cut is by age group, and the group boundaries are the statutory band
  boundaries, so this table is circular for the partition question. Recorded as the reason the
  carrier drops from four of four to three of four.**

- **Richard Dickens, Rebecca Riley and David Wilkinson**, The UK minimum wage at 22 years of age:
  a regression discontinuity approach, *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society Series A* 177(1),
  2014, pp. 95-114. Abstract verbatim: *"A regression discontinuity approach is used to analyse
  the effect of the legislated increase in the UK national minimum wage that occurs at age 22
  years on various labour market outcomes"*, reporting *"an increase of 3-4 percentage points in
  the rate of employment of low skilled individuals"* with unemployment falling among men and
  inactivity among women. **ABSTRACT ONLY, the full text is behind a paywall. Cited to establish
  that a discontinuity at the statutory birthday has been estimated by others, and that the wage
  jump this line needs is their first stage rather than their reported result.**
  The 2011 Sussex working paper version, WPS 21-2011, is free and its abstract carries one
  sentence the journal abstract does not: *"We find no such effect before the NMW was introduced
  and no robust impacts at age 21 or 23 years"*, on Labour Force Survey data. **That is the
  placebo structure this line's carrier needs, already run, on employment rather than on the
  pay floor. The full text was not retrieved this round.**

- **John Jeffrey-Cook**, William Pitt and his Taxes, *British Tax Review* 2010(4), offprint at
  taxadvisers.org.uk. *"The local Land Tax Commissioners were to appoint General Commissioners
  (often themselves) to assess income tax"*; the 1798 Triple Assessment computed from the prior
  year's bill, *"if the 1797 tax paid was under 25 pounds, three times that amount was payable;
  if 25 to 30 pounds, three and a half times"*, with the taxpayer able to pay ten per cent of
  annual income instead if that were less; and *"The use of the preceding year's return saved
  administration"*. **SPECIALIST TAX-HISTORY ARTICLE. It establishes that the 1799 income tax ran
  on the Land Tax and assessed-tax machinery, which pre-dated it by a century, so the registration
  point was present, prior, and not built for the tax.**

- **UK Parliament**, War and the coming of income tax, parliament.uk living heritage. *"No one was
  taxed on incomes below 60 pounds"*; *"those between 60 and 200 pounds were taxed at a graduated
  rate from just under one per cent to 10 per cent"*; all incomes over 200 pounds at ten per cent;
  and *"At first the tax was highly unpopular and was widely evaded, particularly among merchants
  and manufacturers"*. **OFFICIAL PARLIAMENTARY PAGE, used for the 1799 rate structure.**

- **Poll taxes of 1376-1381**, en.wikipedia.org. 1377: flat, one groat (4d) per person aged over
  14, beggars exempt, covering *"almost 60% of the population"*, yielding 22,000 pounds. 1379:
  graduated across seven social ranks, aged 16 and over, yielding 18,600 pounds against an
  expected 50,000, with *"Widespread evasion proved to be a problem"*. 1381: minimum 4d with
  collectors required to account for a mean of 12d per head, expected 66,666 pounds, and
  *"many in the south-east evaded it by refusing to register"*; new commissioners appointed in
  March 1381 whose *"extraordinary powers and interference"* are credited among the main causes
  of the Peasants' Revolt. **TERTIARY SOURCE. The three-tax comparison it supports is the
  strongest reading B33 has, so the per-rank amounts for 1379 and the 60 per cent coverage figure
  for 1377 are marked unverified in the results file and must be taken to a primary edition
  (Fenwick's edition of the returns) before either is quoted outside this line.**

- **Richard Dickens, Rebecca Riley and David Wilkinson**, The UK Minimum Wage at Age 22: A
  Regression Discontinuity Approach, University of Sussex Economics Working Paper 21-2011.
  **FULL TEXT READ.** Table 1, the adult and development rates from April 1999 to October 2008
  and the resulting jump at age 22, between `15.6` and `20.2` per cent. Table 3, Labour Force
  Survey, low skilled, share of employees paid less than the adult minimum from age 18 to 23:
  `29, 19, 15, 10, 6, 5` per cent. Table 4, Annual Survey of Hours and Earnings, all employees,
  same quantity: `30, 20, 13, 9, 3, 1` per cent. Table 5, the employment discontinuity at 22 from
  `0.025` to `0.042`, six of eight specifications significant. Table 6, the same model on
  January 1994 to December 1998 before the minimum wage existed, all discontinuities
  insignificant, one of twenty-four significant at ten per cent and that specification failing
  the goodness of fit test. Tables 7 and 8, at ages 21 and 23, *"no robust evidence of a
  significant discontinuity"*. On resolution: the LFS has *"just over a thousand"* 21 year olds
  per quarter, earnings questions reach about forty per cent of respondents, hourly rate
  information exists for `13` per cent of employees aged 21 or 22, there are about `300`
  individuals at each age in weeks, and *"any examination of wages by week or even month of birth
  is subject to lots of noise. Consequently, we focus our analysis on wage changes by age in
  years."* On measurement error: five per cent of 23 year olds are recorded below the legal
  minimum in the LFS against one per cent in ASHE, which *"suggests a significant degree of
  measurement error in the LFS wage data"*. On confounds: *"The main changes in the benefit
  system happen to individuals at age 18 and then at 25."* **Their outcome variable is employment
  and this line's is the pay floor, so their discontinuity estimates enter as a same-shape result
  on a different outcome, not as this station's reading. Tables 3 and 4 are this station's
  reading.**

# B30-4, chunk one: the carrier is settled before any data is pulled, and one candidate is closed on paper

**Written 2026-09-03. No data was collected for this chunk. Everything below is a
reading of what the arm asks for against what two candidate carriers contain, which
is the same order of work that closed B30-1's first carrier for the price of one
graph and no collection at all.**

## What the arm asks for

Registered in [`b30_prereg.md`](b30_prereg.md) §2:

> **B30-4  comparables against dispersion.** Dispersion of a good's export price
> against the **density of asserted comparables at the destination**.
>
> - dispersion rises as comparables thin out → no anchor to attach to
> - flat → anchoring is not what sets the entry price

**The treatment variable is a property of the destination market: how many things
a seller arriving there could price against.** That is a fact about market
structure, and the arm turns on it being measurable as such.

## The candidate that is closed here, and why

**A city-by-item price matrix was considered and is closed.** The archive behind
B38 carries 53 items across 4,956 cities, and the obvious mapping is: comparables
density becomes **how many of the 53 items a city carries**, and dispersion becomes
the size of that city's two-way residual.

**The mapping does not hold, and the reason is not a data problem.**

**How many items a city carries is a property of the collection, not of the
market.** A city missing thirty rows is a city nobody submitted thirty prices for.
**It is not a city where thirty kinds of goods are unavailable to price against.**
The arm's treatment variable is market structure; the proposed proxy is coverage of
the survey. **They are different objects that happen to be counted the same way.**

**A second reason, independent of the first and enough on its own.** Thin coverage
and thin sampling move together in this archive, so a residual that grows as
coverage falls has at least two roads to it, and B38d already measured that the
thin half's residual is the larger one (`0.4771` against `0.4279`). **Running B30-4
on this carrier would re-read B38d's number and attach a different name to it.**

**Cost of finding this out: one paragraph.**

## The carrier that does hold

**It is the one this file opens on.** The subscription-platform class in the first
arm has the structure B30-4 needs and the city matrix lacks:

| what the arm needs | what the platform class supplies |
|---|---|
| a destination market | one platform |
| density of comparables there | **how many creators sit in the same category on that platform** |
| the price to read | the posted entry tier |
| dispersion | spread of entry tiers within a category |

**Category membership on these platforms is a structural fact rather than a survey
artefact**: a creator is in a category because the platform put them there, and the
count of a category is the count of the market, not the count of what anyone
collected.

**And it lands on a question the first arm leaves open.** That arm established
that the grid **binds where it is not enforced**: Substack posts a floor and no
ceiling, Patreon posts neither, and both sit on the same number as the platform
that enforces it absolutely. **B30-4 asks the next question down: does the grid
still bind where there is almost nothing to copy from?** The first branch says the
spread widens as a category thins; the second says it does not, and then the grid
is doing the work by itself.

## What is not established here, and it is the whole remaining question

**Availability has not been checked.** No source class has been enumerated, nothing
has been pulled, and no claim is made in either direction about whether
category-level creator counts and entry tiers can be obtained.

**Under `D32` that check is a list rather than one lookup**, and it is registered
here as the gate this arm has to pass before anything else: platform-published
category pages and directories, platform APIs, third-party trackers, published
industry analyses, press releases and earnings material, and trade press with its
own tabulations. **A miss in one class is not a miss.**

**So B30-4's state after this chunk is: carrier decided, one candidate closed on
paper, availability gate registered and unrun.** That is a smaller claim than
running the arm, and it was bought for the price of reading two registrations
against each other.

---

# B30-3, chunk one: two cars from opposite ends of the earth move together, and freight is out

**Read 2026-09-03 on the carrier already on disk. No collection, no new series.**

## What the arm asks

> **B30-3  destination ladder against freight.** For one exported good in two
> destinations, the multiple over its home price.
>
> - tracks the destination's own price ladder → the destination anchor wins
> - tracks freight and duty → cost explains it and anchoring is not needed

## The instrument, and why this carrier answers half the arm for free

The 53-item matrix carries **two named cars with different countries of origin**:
`x34` Volkswagen Golf 1.5 and `x35` Toyota Corolla Sedan 1.6. **Both are imported
almost everywhere the archive reaches, and they are imported from opposite ends of
the earth.**

That is a control the arm did not ask for and does not have to buy. **Freight and
distance are properties of the origin.** If cost is what sets the multiple, the
German car should be dear where Germany is far and the Japanese car dear where
Japan is far, **so the two residuals should separate by origin, not move together.**

## The reading

Two-way residual on the complete 53 by 1,280 matrix, then the correlation of the
two car rows across cities.

| | |
|---|---|
| **the two cars' residuals** | **`r = +0.8639`** |
| rank among all 1,378 item pairs | **3rd**, quantile `0.9978` |
| all pairs, median | `-0.0376` |
| all pairs, 90th percentile | `+0.3394` |
| all pairs, 99th percentile | `+0.7064` |

**The five pairs around it are all rent**: one-bedroom against three-bedroom, city
centre against outside, in every combination.

> **Two cars built on opposite sides of the planet deviate together in a city about
> as tightly as two apartment sizes in the same building do.**

**And the baseline says this is not an artefact of everything correlating with
everything.** The median pair reads `-0.0376`. Whatever is holding the two cars
together is specific.

## What this rules out, and the control was free

**Freight and distance are out as the main driver.** They act through origin, the
two origins are as far apart as origins get, and the two residuals are near the top
of the whole matrix's correlation distribution. **A cost account carried by shipping
predicts separation and the data gives the opposite.**

## What it does not rule out, and this is half of the same branch

**Duty is levied by category, not by origin.** A country taxing new cars at a
hundred per cent lifts both of these rows at once, **which produces exactly this
correlation with no destination anchor anywhere in it.** The same holds for
registration tax, dealer structure, and any category-level local charge.

**So the second branch is refuted on freight and untouched on duty**, and those two
were one branch in the registration. **They are separated here because the carrier
happened to separate them.**

## What separates the rest, registered before it is run

**Import duty on passenger cars by country**, from published tariff schedules. The
reading is then a single question with two ways to fail:

- residual tracks the duty schedule → **cost explains it, on the half freight did not**
- residual does not track it → **the destination anchor holds, and the arm returns
  its first branch on a carrier where the alternative was measured rather than
  assumed**

## Limits

1. **This is one reading on one carrier, not a closure.**
2. **Both rows sit in one category**, so any category-level local charge is
   confounded with the anchor and the duty pull is the way to separate them.
3. **The city effect has already absorbed the level and the currency**, so what the
   residual carries is each car's deviation relative to the rest of that city's
   basket, which is the object the arm wants.
4. **Neither row is a home price.** The arm as registered reads a multiple over the
   good's home price; this chunk reads a deviation from the city's own ladder
   instead. **They answer the same question and they are not the same statistic**,
   and the registered form still needs the home-market row.

---

# B30-2, chunk one: the carrier condition, worked out before the census is built

**Written 2026-09-03. No data. This is the arithmetic the registration's "needs a
route census" line stands on.**

## What the count is

From the propagation file and repeated in the general theory:

```
independent checks available = (edge-disjoint routes between the regions) - 1
```

**Menger's theorem counts it**: the maximum number of edge-disjoint paths between
two nodes equals the minimum edge cut separating them. **A region joined to the
rest by a single intermediary has zero checks**, and the relation there is not
unmeasured but unfalsifiable, because there is no second path, so no cycle, so
nothing for Theorem 1 to test.

## Why the census cannot be built on the obvious network

**On a dense graph the count degenerates into degree.** The minimum cut between two
nodes is bounded by the smaller of their degrees, and in a dense network it
typically equals it. **International merchandise trade is dense**: most countries
trade with most countries, so the edge-disjoint count between almost any pair would
return the smaller degree, **and degree in that network tracks economic size.**

**So the arm would read size and report checkability.** That is a confound the
registration's first branch cannot survive, and it costs nothing to see.

## What the carrier has to have

**A sparse network whose edges are physical and countable, containing regions that
genuinely have one route in and out.** The variable has to have a floor that is
actually occupied, because the arm's sharpest cell is the region with a single
intermediary.

Three families meet that shape on their face, and they are recorded as directions
rather than as choices:

- **inland waterway**, where locks and river segments are the edges and a barge has
  no alternative path around a closed lock;
- **pipeline**, where the network is sparse by construction and the routes are
  published;
- **single-port islands and landlocked states**, where the count is one by geography.

**The first of these is already inside the project.** B41's carrier reads a river
against inland positions, and B42 tried lock tonnage as a carrier and recorded why
it did not fit that arm. **Neither of those was built for B30-2**, and no claim is
made here that either supplies the census. **What is claimed is that the sparse
family is where the census can exist at all, and that the dense one is closed.**

## State after this chunk

**Carrier condition stated, the obvious candidate closed on arithmetic, three
directions recorded, census unbuilt.** No route was counted here.

---

# B30-3, chunk two: duty is measured, it enters, and taking all of it out leaves the two cars still moving together

**Read 2026-09-03. Tariffs pulled from UNCTAD TRAINS through the WITS API, HS
870323 (spark-ignition passenger cars, 1500 to 3000 cc), MFN applied as reported,
all reporters against the world.** Record `data/raw/wits_tariff_hs870323.csv`,
190 reporters. **Provenance and the two caveats on it are in `data/SOURCES.md`.**

## Why this chunk exists

Chunk one ruled freight out with a control the carrier supplied for free: two cars
whose origins are on opposite sides of the earth deviate together at `+0.8639`,
third of all 1,378 item pairs. **It left duty standing**, because duty is levied by
category and would lift both rows at once, producing the same correlation with no
destination anchor in it. **This chunk measures duty and takes it out.**

## Coverage

| | |
|---|---|
| countries matched to a tariff | **133 of 149** |
| cities covered | **1,231 of 1,280**, 96 per cent |
| tariff range across matched countries | **0 to 167 per cent** |

Matching is by reporter name under two rules, both of them in the script: **customs
union members not listed individually take the union's common external rate**, and
a short alias table covers naming differences.

## The tariff enters, and it is not a proxy for being poor

| | car rows | rent rows, as control |
|---|---|---|
| Pearson against tariff | **`+0.3686`** | `-0.1555` |
| Pearson against `log(1+t)` | **`+0.3979`** | `-0.1851` |
| **Spearman** | **`+0.6011`** | **`-0.4315`** |

**The control is the point of that table.** If the tariff were standing in for
national income or for a general price level, rent would move with it. **Rent moves
against it, and by a lot.** So what the tariff is picking up in the car rows is
specific to the car rows.

**Elasticities, on `log(1+t)`**: Golf `+0.9291`, Corolla `+1.4778`; variance shares
`0.1386` and `0.3084`.

## The reading this chunk was built for

| | |
|---|---|
| the two cars' residuals, **before** the tariff is removed | **`+0.8606`** |
| the two cars' residuals, **after** regressing each on `log(1+t)` | **`+0.8471`** |

> **Removing the entire measured tariff moves the two cars' co-movement by
> `0.0135`.**

**Duty was the standing account after chunk one, and it is now measured, on the
variable with the widest spread available (zero to 167 per cent). It enters, and it
does not carry what holds the two rows together.**

**Both branches of B30-3's second reading have now been answered on this carrier,
and each by a different device**: freight by an origin control the carrier already
contained, duty by an external schedule pulled and regressed out. **The arm returns
its first branch: the destination's own ladder is what the multiple tracks.**

## The asymmetry between the two cars, named because it is a defect in this chunk

**Corolla's tariff elasticity is `1.478` against Golf's `0.929`, and its variance
share is more than double.** The likely cause is in the matching rule rather than
in the world: **EU members are assigned the union's external rate of 10 per cent,
and that is right for a Japanese car and wrong for a German one**, which moves
inside the union at zero. **So Golf's tariff is overstated across a large block of
matched countries and its relationship is diluted by construction.**

**This works against the chunk's own reading rather than for it**: correcting it
would raise Golf's tariff loading, not lower it, and the co-movement result already
survives the uncorrected version. **It is registered as the first fix.**

## Limits

1. **Duty is not the whole of category-level cost.** Registration tax, excise and
   dealer margin are levied by category too and are not controlled here. **What is
   established is that the widest-spread member of that family does not carry the
   co-movement**, not that no member of it could.
2. **Tariff year is 2020 and the price vintage is late 2022.** MFN rates on
   passenger cars move slowly, and the 2022 column is carried in the same file for
   the 154 reporters that have one, but the reading above uses 2020 for coverage.
   **Re-running on 2022 is registered.**
3. **Sixteen countries and 49 cities have no tariff match**, including Egypt, which
   sits at an extreme of the first principal direction in B38.
4. **`log(1+t)` is a functional form choice.** Both the level and the log are
   printed above and they agree in sign and in order of magnitude.

---

# B30-3, chunk three: the duty control rebuilt in the rival's favour, and the co-movement survives it

**Read 2026-09-03, immediately after chunk two and on the same data.** Chunk two
named a defect in its own matching rule and recorded that correcting it would work
against its reading. **This chunk corrects it, and then goes further than the
correction: it gives the duty account every advantage the data can be made to give
it, and asks whether the reading still stands.**

## First, the route that was tried and does not work

TRAINS carries partner-specific rates, so the exact instrument would be **each
country's applied rate on cars from Germany for the Golf and from Japan for the
Corolla**, with every preferential agreement already inside it. **The API returns
almost nothing at that level**: `partner=276` (Germany) yields **0 reporter series**
and `partner=392` (Japan) yields **10**, against 180 for `partner=000`.
`aveestimated` and `bound` return zero for both. **So the per-origin rate is
constructed by hand instead, and every hand-made element is listed below.**

## The correction, and it is built to favour duty

Duty needs a per-origin rate to be able to explain anything the world rate cannot.
Each of the following **lowers** one car's tariff and therefore **raises** duty's
ability to account for the two rows separately:

| adjustment | who | why |
|---|---|---|
| Golf duty set to **0** | EU members, **Turkey** | customs union, the car moves inside it |
| Golf duty set to **0** | **South Korea** | Korea-EU FTA |
| Golf duty set to **0** | **United Kingdom** | UK-EU agreement |
| Corolla duty set to the **EPA step** | EU members and the UK | EU-Japan EPA phases the car line down from 10 |

**The EPA step is not pinned to a single number, and that is deliberate.** Its exact
value in the tariff year is a fact this station has not verified, **so it is swept
across the whole plausible range and every point is printed.** The reading then does
not depend on getting that number right.

## The result

Both per-origin tariffs are entered together, so anything either schedule can
explain in either row is removed from both.

| control | the two cars' residual correlation |
|---|---|
| none | **`+0.8606`** |
| chunk two: one world rate, uncorrected | `+0.8471` |
| per-origin, EPA step **`0.0`** | **`+0.8241`** |
| per-origin, EPA step `4.0` | `+0.8382` |
| per-origin, EPA step `5.5` | `+0.8431` |
| per-origin, EPA step `6.2` | `+0.8446` |
| per-origin, EPA step `8.3` | `+0.8475` |
| per-origin, EPA step `10.0` | `+0.8488` |
| **per-origin plus both squares plus their interaction, six regressors** | **`+0.8032`** |

**The sweep is monotone in the right direction**: the lower the Japanese car's
European rate, the more the two schedules differ, the more duty can explain, and the
lower the remainder. **That is the model absorbing tariff structure as it should.**
**And the strongest point of the whole sweep is `+0.8241`.**

## Why this is the closing reading

**The last row is the rival's best case that this carrier can construct**: each car
on its own origin's schedule, both schedules entered for both rows, plus curvature
in each and an interaction between them. **Six regressors, and the co-movement goes
from `0.8606` to `0.8032`.**

Set that against the distribution it lives in. **Across all 1,378 item pairs in this
matrix the median is `-0.0376`, the 90th percentile is `+0.3394`, and the 99th is
`+0.7064`.**

> **After duty is given everything, the two cars are still above the 99th percentile
> of the entire matrix.**

**So the second branch is answered on both of its halves and by two different
devices**: freight by an origin control the carrier already contained, duty by an
external schedule pulled, corrected in duty's favour, swept, and entered
non-linearly. **B30-3 returns its first branch, and the alternative was measured at
its strongest rather than assumed away.**

## Limits

1. **The per-origin rates are hand-built**, because the API does not serve them.
   Every element is in the table above and in the script; **none of them is an
   estimate of a price, all of them are statements about which agreement applies.**
2. **Agreements outside the four listed are not applied.** CPTPP, the Japan-UK
   agreement after 2021, and various bilateral arrangements would each lower one
   car's rate somewhere. **Every one of them omitted works in the same direction as
   the others: applying more of them gives duty more power**, and the sweep's
   lowest-remainder end already shows what that does, which is `+0.8241`.
3. **Registration tax, excise and dealer margin remain uncontrolled**, as in chunk
   two. Duty is the member of that family with the widest spread and the only one
   published country by country.
4. **The tariff year is 2020 against a late-2022 price vintage**, unchanged from
   chunk two.

---

# B30-21, chunk two: the window is registered, the floor is measured, and the decay names the mechanism

**Read 2026-09-03. Series pulled through a browser session from the Yahoo Finance
chart endpoint, `GD.AT` for the ATHEX composite and `GREK` for the control, 88
sessions from 2015-05-29 to 2015-09-30, stored at
`data/raw/athex_grek_2015.csv`.** ATHEX carries no value on any of the 23 shut
sessions, which is the first check this file wanted. **Both legs are read on
unadjusted closes**, and the resulting control return over the closure matches the
`19.27` per cent the first chunk reported off adjusted closes, so no distribution
fell inside the window.

## What the first chunk's `NEXT` line asked for

Read the local leg as an average over the first `N` sessions after reopening, `N`
fixed in advance, **so that one session's own swing does not carry the reading**,
and then the floor is the dispersion of that average and is measurable.

**Registered `N` is 5, one trading week.** The whole sweep from one to twenty is
printed because a single window is a choice and the shape of the sweep is the
object.

## The floor, measured

Daily log excess, ATHEX minus GREK, on the **42 common sessions after reopening**:
**sd `2.326` per cent**. The floor for a mean of `N` of them is `sd/sqrt(N)`.

## The readings

Base is 2015-06-26 on both legs. Local and control are log changes to the mean of
the first `N` post-reopening sessions.

| `N` | local | control | **excess** | floor | multiples of floor |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | `-17.71` | `-21.41` | **`+3.69`** | `2.33` | `1.59` |
| 2 | `-18.32` | `-20.78` | `+2.45` | `1.64` | `1.49` |
| 3 | `-19.38` | `-21.66` | `+2.28` | `1.34` | `1.70` |
| **5, registered** | `-18.51` | `-21.37` | **`+2.86`** | `1.04` | **`2.75`** |
| 10 | `-16.54` | `-18.15` | `+1.61` | `0.74` | `2.19` |
| 15 | `-17.18` | `-18.12` | `+0.94` | `0.60` | `1.56` |
| 20 | `-19.41` | `-19.71` | **`+0.30`** | `0.52` | **`0.57`** |

## What this settles, and it is not what either earlier pass said

**The reading is above its floor and the earlier "below resolution" verdict is
withdrawn.** At the registered window it stands at `2.75` multiples. **The floor
used in the second pass was the reopening day's own intraday range, and that is a
statement about how turbulent that day was rather than about how precise this
statistic is.**

**And the sign is positive at every window, which is against the prediction.**

**But the sweep says what is producing it.** The excess decays monotonically as the
window lengthens, from `+3.69` at one session to `+0.30` at twenty, and by twenty
it is at `0.57` of its own floor.

> **A restriction effect does not decay as you average more of the post-event
> window. A control that overshot while the local market was shut and then
> converged does exactly that.**

**That was the competing explanation this arm registered in its first chunk and
declared untested.** It is no longer untested: **the decay is its signature, and it
is in the data.**

## The ruling

**The arm is not a refutation of the class B prediction and it is not a
confirmation.** What it measures on this carrier is the control's own behaviour
during a closure. **The registered replacement stands and is now the only way
forward**: a control without a closed underlying, which the first chunk already
named as depositary receipts, cross-listings, or a regional index excluding the
closed country.

**The C cell of the four-cell reading stays empty**, and this arm is no longer
queued for it.

## Limits

1. **One episode.** `n` is still one.
2. **The floor is estimated on post-reopening sessions only**, because the local
   leg does not exist during the closure. A pre-closure floor would be a different
   number, and both would be estimates of a quiet-period dispersion this episode
   does not contain.
3. **The currency correction is still not computed.** GREK is priced in dollars and
   ATHEX in euro. **It shifts every row in the table by roughly the same amount**,
   so it moves the level of the excess and not the decay, which is what the ruling
   turns on.
4. **`N = 5` was registered before the sweep was printed**, and the sweep is
   reported whole so the choice can be checked against it.

---

# B30-4, chunk two: the availability gate returns a constant where the arm needs a variable

**Run 2026-09-03. Nothing was collected. Six source classes were enumerated and
tried, per `D32`, and the finding is not that the data is hard to get.**

## What the arm needs

Chunk one settled the carrier: the subscription-platform class, where a category's
creator count is a structural fact rather than a survey artefact. **The treatment
variable is that count.** The arm reads dispersion of entry price against it.

## The six classes, and what each returned

| class | tried | returned |
|---|---|---|
| 1. platform's own directory | Patreon `/explore` | **not reachable from this session's browser**, which is a statement about this instrument and not about the world |
| 2. platform API | Substack `/api/v1/categories` | **works**, returns the category table. **And its own description says each category's leaderboard is the top 25** |
| 3. third-party tracker | Graphtreon | top 1,000 platform-wide, **no category and no entry tier**. Its per-patron figure is revenue divided by members across all tiers, which is not the entry price |
| 4. published industry analysis | Contrary Research and the statistics aggregators | platform-level totals, **never a per-category distribution** |
| 5. press and financial material | searched | same, and the hits are secondary compilations of the same totals |
| 6. trade-press tabulation | Newsletter Circle's "75k newsletters analysed" | **an announcement rather than a report.** Categories and pricing are listed as forthcoming, the dataset is not published |

## The finding, and it is a gate rather than a shortage

**Class 2 is the one that matters, and it does not fail by returning nothing.** It
returns **25**, for every category.

**A leaderboard is a platform's display rule, not a market's size.** Every category
on Substack shows the same depth because the platform chose that depth. **So on this
route the treatment variable takes exactly one value.**

> **That is gate zero (`D18`), and the answer is one.** Not "the sample is small":
> **the variable has no variation on this observation unit at all.**

**Under gate zero's 2026-08-27 clause the next question is whether the variable
varies on some other observation unit** — language, region, sub-tag. **Each of
those needs the same thing the category route needs: a full count. And a full count
is precisely what none of the six classes contains.**

## What would open it, stated so the next attempt is not a repeat

**One published number: how many creators sit in a category.** Any of the following
supplies it and none of them was found:

- a platform that publishes category sizes in its own directory;
- a dataset, public or purchasable, with a per-category census rather than a ranking;
- a filing or report that discloses the distribution rather than the total.

**Until one exists, the arm's treatment variable is not measurable on this carrier
family, and the reading it would produce would be about display depth.**

## State

**Carrier settled (chunk one), availability gate run across six classes and
returned a constant (this chunk), arm not opened.** **Total collection cost across
both chunks: zero.**

**One thing this chunk does not claim.** Patreon's directory was unreachable from
this session, and that is an instrument limit rather than a finding. **If it
becomes reachable it should be checked**, because it is the one class-1 source that
was never actually read.

# B30-23, chunk fourteen: the channel changes to exchange fee schedules, and the census returns twenty-seven retirements

**Read 2026-09-03** against the design file's section 3c. Script
`experiments/b30_23_sro_census.py`, record `results/b30_23_sro_census.json`, input
`data/raw/fr_sec/` collected by `data/fetch_sro_filings.py`. **No fee schedule has
been retrieved. This block establishes the population and names the seams.**

## Why the channel moved

Two negatives on insurance rate filings had a structural cause, stated in chunk
eleven: **a filed rating plan is observable because it is lodged per entity, and
being lodged per entity is what stops it moving.** The selection rule that followed
ranks four channels by whether the incumbent procedure can survive, and asks for an
industry where the operating entity itself is sold and continues.

**A US exchange is that.** Its fee schedule is published and tiered, every amendment
is filed under Rule 19b-4 and printed in the Federal Register with a date, **and when
an exchange licence is retired its fee filings stop and its members are priced by
another exchange's schedule.** The procedure loses its host.

## The index, and what parsing it cost

**44,604 notices issued by the Commission across 2005 to 2026**, title and date only,
one file per month, resumable. **32,059 are self-regulatory notices.**

**Four title shapes, and the fourth was found by printing the names rather than
counting them.** Three put the entity in its own semicolon segment. The oldest
separate it from the action with a comma, so a raw count of the second segment reads
**391 distinct entities where there are 162**. The tail of that list is where the
comma shape shows: entries reading `new york stock exchange, inc., notice of filing of
proposed rule change and amendment nos...`. **The top of the list looks perfect.**

**Dropped and named rather than silently absorbed**: 12,545 not self-regulatory,
1,068 with the entity inside the action clause, 109 naming several entities at once.

## The reading

| | |
|---|---|
| notices read | **44,604** |
| distinct entities after normalising | **162** |
| entities with at least 25 notices | **68** |
| **of those, entities whose filings have stopped** | **27** |

**The 68 is the first gate's answer and it was not guessed.** How many carriers this
channel can offer is what the census returns, which is why it runs before a case is
named.

**The seams line up with known transfers to within weeks:**

| retired | last filing | successor | first filing | gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| philadelphia stock exchange | 2008-09-02 | nasdaq omx phlx | 2008-09-24 | +22d |
| american stock exchange | 2008-12-10 | nyse amex | 2009-04-06 | +117d |
| boston stock exchange | 2009-02-27 | nasdaq omx bx | 2009-01-26 | −32d |
| edgx exchange | 2016-04-12 | bats edgx exchange | 2016-03-23 | −20d |
| international securities exchange | 2017-04-04 | nasdaq ise | 2017-04-17 | +13d |
| national stock exchange | 2017-02-08 | nyse national | 2017-04-14 | +65d |
| c2 options exchange | 2017-11-03 | cboe c2 exchange | 2017-11-27 | +24d |
| bats byx exchange | 2017-11-16 | cboe byx exchange | 2017-11-16 | 0d |
| chicago stock exchange | 2019-02-07 | nyse chicago | 2019-03-01 | +22d |

**Negative gaps are overlap, not error**: the successor files before the predecessor
stops, which is the transition period.

## Pairing is by name, not by date, and the reason is in the data

**The script prints every entity starting within two years of a retirement and pairs
none of them.** In 2016 and 2017 whole families were renamed at once, six Cboe and
five Nasdaq entities, so a date window returns twenty candidates a row and separates
nothing.

**The names do separate them, because the acquired identifier survives inside the new
name**: `nasdaq omx phlx`, `nyse chicago`, `cboe c2 exchange`, `bats edgx exchange`.
**That survival is itself a reading**: the licence is retired and the mark is kept.

**Matching on a shared root pairs 19 of the 27 and leaves 8 named.** Of those eight,
**five are false negatives with two named causes**, and both were found by printing
the eight rather than the nineteen:

- **abbreviation**: `philadelphia` to `phlx`, `american` to `amex`,
  `international securities exchange` to `ise`, `boston` to `bx`
- **the stop-word list ate a load-bearing word**: `national` was treated as generic,
  so `national stock exchange` and `nyse national` share no root under it

**Three are real**: `pacific exchange`, `topaz exchange` and the dealers' association
each stop without their mark surviving anywhere. **The fourth, a clearing house, has
no successor at all.**

**No further automation.** Pairing is not the criterion; the criterion is what happens
to the count of distinct class values. **Nineteen paired and eight named is more
carriers than the criterion needs**, and driving the matcher to completion buys
nothing the criterion will use.

## What the next block has to decide, and the trap it has to avoid

**The seams split two ways and only one of them is a carrier.**

| | example | is it a carrier |
|---|---|---|
| **rebrand inside one owner** | nyse amex to nyse mkt to nyse american; nasdaq omx phlx to nasdaq phlx; chicago board options exchange to cboe exchange | **no.** Two versions of one program, and this is exactly the shape that produced a withdrawn verdict on the previous channel |
| **acquisition across owners** | philadelphia to nasdaq; american to nyse; edgx to bats to cboe; ise to nasdaq; chicago to nyse; pacific to nyse arca | **yes.** The acquirer arrives with a fee schedule of its own |

**The split cannot be inferred from the names**, because an abbreviation makes a
rebrand look like an acquisition: `chicago board options exchange` to `cboe exchange`
shares no root and is one owner throughout.

**The first attempt to read it off the titles was wrong, and it was caught the same
hour by running it.** The proposal was: 86 notices in the index announce a name change
in their own titles, so **a retirement accompanied by one of those is a rebrand and a
retirement without one is a candidate acquisition**. Run against the 27 retirements it
splits them 14 and 13, **and the split is not the one claimed**:

> `international securities exchange`, `ise gemini` and `ise mercury` each carry a
> name-change notice of their own, titled **`To Rename the Exchange as Nasdaq ISE`**.
> **Those are renames after an acquisition, by the acquirer.**

**A name-change notice says the legal entity survived under a new name. It says
nothing about whether the owner changed**, because a new owner renames what it buys.
The check also returns two false positives, one of them a notice renaming a *product*
rather than the exchange. **This is the same shape as the withdrawn verdict on the
previous channel: an identifier that looks like it carries provenance and does not.**

**Then the direct evidence was looked for in the titles and it is not there either.**
Across the thirteen cross-owner seams, counting notices in the successor's first year
whose title says harmonise, align, conform, or names an affiliated exchange:
**one hit in thirteen**, and that one harmonises the definition of a non-professional
user rather than replacing a schedule. The same wording appears **516 times across the
index as a whole**, so aligning fees with an affiliate is an ordinary and continuous
activity between exchanges rather than a signature of a transfer.

> **The titles are exhausted. Whether a post-seam filing replaces a schedule or amends
> one, and what the count of distinct class values does, are both in the body of the
> document.**

**Which is where the criterion always was.** The criterion counts distinct class
values in the filed schedule, and no title carries that. **The census's job was to
name the population and the seams, and it has.**

## Limits

1. **Titles only, and that limit is now measured rather than assumed.** Two title-level
   proxies were tried against the 27 retirements and both failed: a name-change notice
   does not separate rebrand from acquisition, and harmonisation wording appears once
   in thirteen seams against 516 times index-wide. **The criterion's own count is not
   readable from a title at all.**
2. **The 25-notice floor is a legibility cut, not a threshold on any reading.** It
   removes stubs and parse remnants. Entities below it are in the record.
3. **Clearing houses and the regulator's own notices sit in the same population** and
   are not separated here. They have fee filings too and some have retirements.
4. **The abbreviation failures above are not repaired.** The eight are named, which
   is what the next block needs.


---

# B30-23, chunk fifteen: one document closes the retrieval route and pays for itself

**Read 2026-09-03.** One Federal Register document was retrieved, `2019-04285`,
`SR-NYSECHX-2019-01`, 12,463 bytes, script `data/fetch_fr_fulltext.py`, on disk at
`data/raw/fr_text/`. **It was retrieved instead of fifty-two because the first
question is whether the object the criterion counts is in this text at all.**

## It is not

The criterion counts **distinct class values in a filed fee schedule**. This document
carries the amendments to one, not the schedule:

- Section E.6, Routing Services Fees: replace all text with `Reserved`
- Section E.8(c): amend the definition of eligible executions
- Section E.9, SNAP Execution Fees: **delete the section in its entirety**
- Section Q, SNAP Incentive Program: **delete the section in its entirety**

**And the document says where the schedule itself is, in its own first section:**

> *"The proposed rule change is available on the Exchange's website at www.nyse.com,
> at the principal office of the Exchange, and at the Commission's Public Reference
> Room."*

**The index is not the document.** Forty-four thousand titles read like a corpus of
filings, and the tiered schedule those filings amend is an exhibit lodged elsewhere.
**The distinct-value count is not in the Federal Register at any depth.**

**Where it is**: the complete filing including its schedule exhibit is posted as a
PDF by each exchange and by the Commission's own SRO pages. That is retrievable, and
the URL shape differs per exchange, **so the cost of the criterion is fifty-two PDFs
in a dozen layouts rather than fifty-two text fetches in one.** Registered; not
started.

## What the one document paid for, and it is a reading

**The same page carries a directional answer to this arm's own question, stated by
the party.** SNAP, the Sub-second Non-displayed Auction Process, was the acquired
exchange's own mechanism. Six months after the transfer closed:

| section | what happened to it |
|---|---|
| E.6 Routing Services Fees | emptied to `Reserved` |
| **E.9 SNAP Execution Fees** | **deleted entirely** |
| **Q SNAP Incentive Program** | **deleted entirely** |

with the functionality *"both decommissioned on December 31, 2018"*.

**Three fee sections of the acquired exchange were removed or emptied, and the two
deleted outright priced a mechanism that existed nowhere else.** The criterion asks
what the count of distinct class values does across a transfer. **This is that count
moving, in the direction of the acquired program being retired, evidenced by the
acquirer's own filing rather than inferred from a name.**

**It is not the criterion scored.** The criterion needs the two counts, and those need
the schedules. **It is one seam's direction, free, from the document that closed the
cheap route.**

## Limits

1. **One document, one seam, one direction.** No count has been taken.
2. **A deleted section is not the same as a reduced value count**: the remaining
   sections could carry more distinct values than before. **The direction here is of
   the acquired mechanism's removal, not of the schedule's cardinality.**
3. **The PDF route is registered and unpriced.** Whether a fee schedule exhibit parses
   into a count is the same question this block asked of the text, and it has to be
   asked once, on one PDF, before fifty-two.

# B30-23, chunk sixteen: the exhibit parses, the schedule is not in it, and the route is priced

**Read 2026-09-03.** One exhibit retrieved, `34-85248-ex5.pdf`, 234 KB, three pages,
the Exhibit 5 accompanying `SR-NYSECHX-2019-01`.

## Three things the one exhibit settles

**The URL shape is regular and scriptable.** The Commission serves the filing and its
exhibits at `files/rules/sro/{exchange}/{year}/{release}-ex5.pdf`, and the release
number is printed in the Federal Register text this project already has on disk, in
the line `[Release No. 34-85248; File No. SR-NYSECHX-2019-01]`. **Two retrievals per
seam-side, both scriptable, no page to search.**

**A declared user agent is required.** Without one the Commission returns a 1,924-byte
page reading `Request Rate Threshold Exceeded`, which saves as a file with a `.pdf`
name and is not a PDF. **A size check catches it; a name check does not.**

**The table survives the conversion.** `pdftotext -layout` holds the columns:

```
  Order Size        Tape        Security Price        Routing Fee
   Odd Lots      All Tapes        All Prices          $0.0040/share
  Round and                     >= $1.00/share        $0.0030/share
  Mixed Lots     All Tapes       < $1.00/share      0.30% of trade value
```

**So counting distinct class values off one of these is a parse, not a research
problem.**

## What it does not contain, and it says so itself

The exhibit is a marked-up amendment: `Proposed additions underlined`, `Proposed
deletions [bracketed]`, and **unchanged sections are represented by the word
`Unchanged`**:

    E. Transaction and Order Processing Fees
           1. - 5. Unchanged
           6. Reserved[Routing Services Fees   ... ]

**A marked-up exhibit prints what moved, not what the schedule is.** The criterion
needs the whole partition on both sides of a seam, and no single amendment carries it.

## And a whole schedule is not findable from the titles either

**Searched on disk, no retrieval**: fee notices whose title says restate, in its
entirety, adopt a new fee schedule, or comprehensive. **69 hits, and printing them
shows almost all are false**: `Consolidated Audit Trail` matched a pattern looking for
a consolidated fee schedule. **The count alone reads as a rich seam; the objects read
as a different subject entirely.** The genuine ones adopt a schedule for a single new
product rather than restating an existing one.

> **Titles gave the population and the seams. They do not give the schedule, and three
> separate attempts to make them do so have now failed.**

## The route, priced

**Two ways to a whole schedule remain, and neither is free.**

| route | what it costs | what it risks |
|---|---|---|
| **the exchange's own site, current schedule plus an archive capture for the historical side** | one capture per seam-side, **plus finding each exchange's historical page URL** | layouts differ per exchange, so the parse is written per exchange |
| **accumulate every marked-up exhibit across the seam** | 88 fee filings on one side of this seam alone and 66 on the other | drift: a hundred amendments summed is a reconstruction, not a reading |

**The first is the cheaper and the honest one.** At the reference repetition count of
five seams rather than all thirteen, it is **ten whole schedules**, and the parse is
the part already shown to work.

## What this arm holds without that step

1. **A population and its size, returned rather than assumed**: 68 entities, 27
   retirements, 13 cross-owner seams.
2. **One seam's direction, stated by the acquirer**: two fee sections of the acquired
   exchange deleted entirely and a third emptied, six months after closing, for a
   mechanism that existed nowhere else.
3. **Three screens tried and refuted, each on the record**: name-change notices do not
   separate rebrand from acquisition; harmonisation wording is ordinary traffic; whole
   schedules are not announced in titles.

**The criterion is not scored and the two counts are the only thing missing.**

## Limits

1. **One exhibit, one seam.** The parse is shown to work on one layout.
2. **`Unchanged` is load-bearing in the exhibit format** and means a count taken off
   an amendment is a count of the amended part, not of the schedule.
3. **The false-positive rate of title screens is now three for three.** Any further
   screen on titles should be assumed to fail until its objects are printed.
