# B30-23, chunk seventeen: the whole schedules are reachable, and the first one shows the law in its plainest form yet

**Read 2026-09-03.** Two whole fee schedules on disk under `data/raw/fr_text/`:
the acquired exchange's own schedule as captured 2017-08-26, 28 pages, `Updated
through July 27, 2017`, eleven months before the transfer closed; and one of the
acquirer's exchanges, current, 12 pages. **The retrieval question is closed.**

## Where a whole schedule lives, settled

| | |
|---|---|
| **the current side** | the exchange publishes it at `publicdocs/nyse/regulation/nyse/{name}_Schedule_of_Fees.pdf`, 12 pages, **no markup, no `Unchanged`** |
| **the historical side** | the retired exchange's own site, through a web archive, **and the capture is a whole PDF rather than a page of links** |

**The archive index was queried before anything was fetched**, which named the
captures and their sizes: the acquired exchange's schedule was captured at 200 with
380 KB in June and August 2017, and the page that linked to it turns 301 in April
2018, **which is itself the retirement showing up in the archive**.

## The acquired exchange's first table, and it is the counting law with nothing in the way

Section E.1, executions from single-sided round-lot orders:

```
 Trading      Tape    Security Price    Liquidity Providing   Liquidity Removing
 Session                                     Credit                  Fee
             Tape A   >= $1.00/share      $0.0020/share         $0.0030/share
                      <  $1.00/share      $0.00009/share      0.30% of trade value
   All       Tape B   >= $1.00/share      $0.0020/share         $0.0030/share
 Trading              <  $1.00/share      $0.00009/share      0.30% of trade value
 Sessions    Tape C   >= $1.00/share      $0.0020/share         $0.0030/share
                      <  $1.00/share      $0.00009/share      0.30% of trade value
```

| | |
|---|---|
| cells drawn | **12** (3 tapes x 2 price classes x 2 sides) |
| **distinct values written** | **4** |

**The three tapes are priced identically cell for cell.** A published procedure draws
three classes on that axis and produces exactly the values one class would produce.
**This is an original document at five significant figures, not a transcription**, so
the collision is not a rounding artefact of the kind that closed the tariff corpus.

**Across the whole of section E**: 23 cells carrying a value, **7 distinct values**.

## Three parse traps, all found by printing the objects

1. **A decimal cap in the pattern manufactured collisions.** A rate pattern capped at
   five decimals turned the six-decimal audit-trail fees `$0.000013`, `$0.000022`,
   `$0.000035`, `$0.000009`, `$0.000002`, `$0.000001` into repeats of one truncated
   token. **The same failure that closed the tariff corpus, reproduced inside the
   parser rather than by the source.**
2. **The audit-trail fees are not this exchange's procedure.** They are set by a
   national market system plan and are identical across every exchange, **so counting
   them makes both sides of a seam look more alike than they are.** The criterion is
   scoped to the transaction-fee section.
3. **A threshold is not a value.** `$1.00/share` appears 18 times in section E and is
   never a fee: it defines the price class. **The tariff corpus separated these into
   two printed columns; here they sit in one flow of text and have to be separated by
   role.** The separation is taken from the table's own structure, the set of values
   appearing after a comparison operator, rather than from a hard-coded number.

## What remains

**One side of the seam.** The successor's whole schedule under the acquirer's brand
has not been located: the URL guessed from the acquirer's current naming returns
nothing in the archive, so its path is being searched. **The acquired-side and one
acquirer-side schedule are both on disk.**

## Limits

1. **Two schedules, one of three the criterion needs.**
2. **Scope is the transaction-fee section on both sides**, chosen so the two are
   comparable and so the plan-set audit-trail fees are excluded. Ports, co-location,
   listing and regulatory fees are outside it.
3. **A cell count taken from a text flow is a count of assigned values, not of grid
   positions**, and the two agree only where every position carries a printed value.
4. **The acquirer is a family of four exchanges** and which of them is the comparison
   is a choice that has to be printed rather than assumed.


---

# B30-23, chunk eighteen: the acquirer tells its own members the procedure is being replaced

**Read 2026-09-03.** `NYSE_Chicago_Migration.pdf`, 260 KB, two pages, dated 18 June
2019, on disk at `data/raw/fr_text/`. **A notice from the acquirer to the acquired
exchange's own traders**, addressed and dated on its face:

    Date:      June 18, 2019
    Audience:  NYSE Chicago Traders
    Subject:   NYSE Pillar Migration Update - NYSE Chicago

## What it says, in its own words

> *"Subject to regulatory approval, NYSE Chicago will migrate to the NYSE Pillar
> Platform on November 4, 2019."*

with, as its own bullets:

- the acquirer's technology platform
- **a price-time priority model**
- **it will not be a primary listing venue and will not conduct auctions in any
  symbols**
- trading operated out of the acquirer's New Jersey data centre

and separately:

> *"NYSE Chicago will eliminate Market Data Revenue ("MDR") rebates on July 1, 2019."*

## Why this is the arm's own question answered in the direction it asks about

**The matching model is the procedure.** The acquired exchange ran a sub-second
non-displayed auction of its own and priced it in its own fee schedule; **the notice
says auctions stop, the model becomes the acquirer's, the venue moves, and a rebate
that lived in the fee schedule is eliminated.** Together with the rule filing that
deleted two fee sections outright, **this seam has three independent pieces of
evidence that the acquired procedure was replaced, every one of them a statement by
the acquirer in a dated document.**

## What it is not

**It is not the criterion scored, and the distinction is worth keeping sharp.** The
criterion counts distinct class values on both sides of the seam. **What is on the
record here is that the procedure was replaced, not by how much the count moved, and
those are different claims.** A schedule can be replaced wholesale and land on the
same cardinality.

**The missing side is the successor's whole schedule.** Its path is not the one
guessed from the acquirer's current naming, and the archive that would resolve it
returned `Internet Archive services are temporarily offline` mid-session.
**Registered, blocked on an external service rather than on a decision.**

## Limits

1. **One seam.** The census names thirteen cross-owner seams and this reads one.
2. **The evidence here is of replacement, not of the count.** Reporting it as the
   criterion would be reporting a different quantity than the one registered.
3. **The notice is about the trading platform and the venue**; the fee schedule
   consequences it states are the MDR rebate and, through the separate filing, the
   auction fee sections. It is not a schedule.

# B30-23, chunk nineteen: seven whole schedules, and the counting law reads off them without a seam

**Read 2026-09-05.** Seven original fee schedules on disk under `data/raw/fr_text/`,
**every one a whole published schedule, none of them an amendment**: four exchanges,
2014 to 2026, 12 to 34 pages each. Retrieved from the exchanges' own sites and, for
the dated ones, from web-archive captures at timestamps already in hand.

## Where they came from, and the naming is in families

**Guessing one path family and stopping cost a day.** The schedules sit under two
different roots with two different naming conventions:

| family | path | naming |
|---|---|---|
| one exchange | `publicdocs/nyse/regulation/nyse/` | `{name}_Schedule_of_Fees.pdf` |
| the others | `publicdocs/nyse/markets/{exchange}/` | `{name}_Equities_Price_List.pdf`, or `NYSE_Price_List.pdf` |

**Two exchanges have no whole schedule under either**, and return a 196-byte error
page that saves under a `.pdf` name.

## The reading

**Scope is set by dimension, not by section heading.** The section structure differs
between schedules and even between two vintages of the same exchange, so a
section-based scope is not comparable. **A per-share rate has a shape no other charge
in these documents has**: three to six decimals of a dollar. Listing fees are
`$X,XXX`, port fees carry `/month`. **The partition is the documents' own, not a
choice.**

| schedule | pages | per-share cells | distinct values | collisions |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NYSE main 2014 | 21 | 72 | 25 | 47 |
| NYSE main 2026 | 34 | 158 | 60 | 98 |
| **NYSE MKT 2015** | 27 | **77** | **16** | **61, 79 per cent** |
| NYSE American 2018 | 26 | 10 | 7 | 3 |
| NYSE American 2026 | 19 | 25 | 19 | 6 |
| NYSE National 2026 | 12 | 20 | 19 | 1 |
| CHX 2017 | 28 | 18 | 7 | 11 |
| **seven schedules** | | **380** | **153** | **227, 59.7 per cent** |

**Close to six in ten cells produce no new value.** These are original documents at
five significant figures with no markup, **which is the property the tariff corpus
lost and the reason its collision count could not be read back onto its sources**.

**The spread across schedules is itself a reading**: one schedule collides on 79 per
cent of its cells and another on 5 per cent. **The law does not predict a rate; it
predicts that the two counts differ and that the second is smaller, and that holds in
all seven.**

**One scope note.** The dimension filter takes only dollar-denominated per-share
rates, so a schedule pricing some cells as a percentage of trade value or per order
has those cells outside the count. CHX 2017 reads 18 cells here against 23 under a
section-based scope for that reason. **The two scopes answer different questions and
neither is wrong; this table uses the one that is comparable across seven documents.**

## The same data supplies the criterion's control, and the control is not favourable

**One exchange appears twice, eight years apart, with no change of owner between:**

| | cells | distinct values |
|---|---|---|
| NYSE American 2018 | 10 | 7 |
| NYSE American 2026 | 25 | 19 |

**One programme, one owner, and the count of distinct values grew by a factor near
three.** So a difference in that count across a transfer cannot carry the claim the
criterion registered: **the programme's own drift over a few years is larger than
anything a transfer needs to produce.**

**This is a finding about the criterion, not about the retrieval**, and it would have
been just as true had the missing schedule been found.

## What the criterion should compare instead

**A procedure that travelled leaves its own values in the other party's schedule.**
That is a statement about **which values**, not about **how many**:

> **Registered form, superseding the count comparison for this arm**: take the set of
> distinct per-share values in B before and after, and in A. **Report the overlap of
> B-after with A, against the overlap of B-after with B-before.** The three outcomes
> of section 1 map onto it unchanged: B moves to A's set, B keeps its own, or a third
> set appears.

**Set overlap is immune to the drift that kills the count**: a schedule can add
fifteen new tiers over eight years and still carry its own historical values, and it
is the values themselves that a transferred procedure would replace. **A criterion
shape corrected after a run is free and needs no flow**, and the reading it replaces
is printed above rather than removed.

## Limits

1. **Seven schedules, four exchanges, one exchange family.** Nothing here is a
   cross-family comparison.
2. **The dimension filter is a scope, and CHX reads differently under the two scopes.**
   Both numbers are printed.
3. **The set-overlap form is registered, not run.** It needs the same three schedules
   the count form needed, and one of them is still missing.
4. **The control is one exchange over one interval.** Two vintages of one schedule is
   not a distribution of drift.


---

---

# B30-23, chunk twenty: the second criterion shape is refuted before the third schedule is fetched, and the arm closes on values

**Read 2026-09-05**, on the seven schedules already on disk. **No retrieval.** The
shape registered in chunk nineteen was put through a reachability check first, and it
does not survive it.

## The check

**Both registered shapes assume one thing**: that A's procedure and B's procedure are
distinguishable in the per-share values they print. **That is testable on schedules
already in hand**, by asking how much two value sets overlap when they are known to be
the same programme and when they are known to be different ones.

Jaccard overlap of the distinct per-share value sets, all twenty-one pairs:

| | pairs | Jaccard |
|---|---|---|
| **same entity, different vintage** | 4 | **0.250 to 0.404** |
| **different exchanges** | 17 | **0.042 to 0.565** |

**The two distributions overlap completely, and the top of the different-exchanges
range sits above the top of the same-entity range.** The highest pair in the whole
table is two different exchanges at `0.565`, higher than any pair of vintages of one
schedule.

**The cause is plain once printed**: per-share rates cluster in a narrow band around
`$0.0010` to `$0.0030`, and exchanges in one family price near each other. **Two
unrelated schedules share values because the quantity has a customary range, not
because a procedure travelled.**

## Both shapes are now refuted, and for different reasons

| shape | how it died |
|---|---|
| **count of distinct values** (registered §1) | **the programme's own drift is larger than the signal**: one exchange, one owner, 7 values to 19 in eight years |
| **overlap of value sets** (registered §1a) | **the two outcomes are not separable on this carrier**: same-programme and different-programme overlaps occupy the same range |

**Neither death is about retrieval.** The missing third schedule would not have changed
either number.

## The assumption they share, and the instrument that does not use it

**Two instruments failing is when the shared assumption gets written down**, not when
the question gets blamed. Both shapes read the procedure **through the values it
prints**. **The values are a customary quantity in a narrow band, so they carry the
industry, not the procedure.**

**The instrument that does not use that assumption reads the structure instead**: how
many tiers, what the tiers are defined on, which mechanisms are priced at all. **And
that instrument has already returned three readings on this seam**, each stated by the
acquirer in a dated document:

- two fee sections of the acquired exchange deleted in their entirety, both pricing a
  mechanism that existed nowhere else
- the matching model replaced with the acquirer's, auctions ended, venue moved
- the listing-fee section emptied to `Reserved`

**None of those is a value comparison.** Each is a statement about what the schedule
prices at all.

## Where this leaves the arm, stated as four separate things

1. **The counting-law reading stands and needs no seam**: 380 per-share cells, 153
   distinct values, 59.7 per cent collisions across seven original schedules
   (chunk nineteen). **That is a reading about published procedures, not about
   transfers.**
2. **The census stands**: 68 entities, 27 retirements, 13 cross-owner seams
   (chunk fourteen).
3. **On one seam the procedure was replaced, and the acquirer says so in three dated
   documents** (chunks fifteen and eighteen). **That is a structural reading, not the
   registered criterion.**
4. **The registered criterion is closed on this carrier in its value form**, both
   shapes refuted above with the arithmetic printed. **A structural form is not
   registered here and would be a different arm.**

**Discipline says a criterion whose outcomes cannot both be reached is not a criterion,
and to confirm both outcomes are reachable before paying.** That confirmation happened
after the collection rather than before, **and it cost the collection**. The check
itself was free and ran on what was already on disk.

## Limits

1. **Twenty-one pairs, one exchange family.** Overlap between families is not measured
   and could be lower.
2. **Four same-entity pairs.** That is a range, not a distribution.
3. **The structural readings in point 3 are one seam and are not a scored criterion.**
